\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}r\lvert\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}=0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\lvert\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\mu^{2}r^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}r.

(2.37)

Proof.

It follows easily by integrating the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{r}((r-r_{+})\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2})=\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2}+2(r-r_{+})\Re(\bar{\varphi}\partial_{r}\varphi)

(2.38)

from
r_{+}
to
r^{\prime}
and applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the last product term.
∎

We will also use the following standard Hardy’s inequality cited from
[
3
, Lemma 4.30]
. Its proof is standard and can be found therein.

Lemma 2.16

(One-dimensional Hardy estimates)
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
and
h:[r_{0},r_{1}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a
C^{1}
function.

(1)

If
r_{0}^{\alpha}|h(r_{0})|^{2}\leq D_{0}
and
\alpha<0
, then

\displaystyle-2\alpha^{-1}r_{1}^{\alpha}|h(r_{1})|^{2}+\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}r^{\alpha-1}|h(r)|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}r^{\alpha+1}|\partial_{r}h(r)|^{2}\mathrm{d}r-2\alpha^{-1}D_{0};

(2.39a)

(2)

If
r_{1}^{\alpha}|h(r_{1})|^{2}\leq D_{0}
and
\alpha>0
, then

\displaystyle 2\alpha^{-1}r_{0}^{\alpha}|h(r_{0})|^{2}+\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}r^{\alpha-1}|h(r)|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\leq\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}r^{\alpha+1}|\partial_{r}h(r)|^{2}\mathrm{d}r+2\alpha^{-1}D_{0}.

(2.39b)

Further, recall the following Sobolev-type estimates from
[
3
, Lemmas 4.32 and 4.33]
where the proof is also provided.

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
\varphi_{s}
be a spin
s
scalar. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\lvert\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{s}{}\lVert\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-1}^{3}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2}.

(2.40)

If
\alpha\in(0,1]
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\lvert\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{s,\alpha}{}(\lVert\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-2}^{3}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2}+\lVert rV\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-1-\alpha}^{2}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\lVert\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-2}^{3}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2}+\lVert rV\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-1+\alpha}^{2}(\Sigma_{\tau})}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(2.41)

If
\lim\limits_{{\tau\to\infty}}\lvert r^{-1}\varphi_{s}\rvert=0
pointwise in
(\rho,\theta,{\tilde{\phi}})
, then

\displaystyle\lvert r^{-1}\varphi_{s}\rvert^{2}\lesssim_{s}{}\lVert\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-3}^{3}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau,\infty})}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\varphi_{s}\rVert_{W_{-3}^{3}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau,\infty})}.

(2.42)

Finally, we provide a lemma showing that a hierarchy of energy and Morawetz estimates implies a rate of decay for the energy in the hierarchy. The way this lemma is stated is the same as
[
3
, Lemma 5.2]
and we have taken the simpler case
\gamma=0
. In applications,
k
represents a level of regularity,
p
represents a weight, and
\tau
represents a time coordinate. Further,
k^{\prime}
characterizes the potential loss of regularity in the hierarchy of energy and Morawetz estimates.

Lemma 2.18

(A hierarchy of energy and Morawetz estimates implies energy decay)
.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
p_{1}\leq p_{2}-1
, let
k^{\prime}\geq 0
, and let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
be suitably large. Let
F:\{0,\ldots,k_{0}\}\times[p_{1}-1,p_{2}]\times[\tau_{0},\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be such that
F(k,p,\tau)
is Lebesgue measurable in
\tau
for each
p
and
k
. Let
D:\{0,\ldots,k_{0}\}\times[p_{1},p_{2}]\times[\tau_{0},\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be such that
D(k,p,\tau)
is Lebesgue measurable in
\tau
for each
p
and
k
.

If

(1)

[monotonicity] for all
k,k_{1},k_{2}\in\{0,\ldots,k_{0}\}
with
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
, all
p,\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in[p_{1},p_{2}]
with
\beta_{1}\leq\beta_{2}
, and all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
,

\displaystyle F(k_{1},p,\tau)\lesssim{}

\displaystyle F(k_{2},p,\tau),

(2.43a)

\displaystyle F(k,\beta_{1},\tau)\lesssim{}

\displaystyle F(k,\beta_{2},\tau),

(2.43b)

and the same for
D(k,p,\tau)
,

(2)

[interpolation] for all
k\in\{0,\ldots,k_{0}\}
, all
p,\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in[p_{1},p_{2}]
such that
\beta_{1}\leq p\leq\beta_{2}
, and all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
,

\displaystyle F(k,p,\tau)\lesssim{}

\displaystyle F(k,\beta_{1},\tau)^{\frac{\beta_{2}-p}{\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}}}F(k,\beta_{2},\tau)^{\frac{p-\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}}},

(2.43c)

(3)

[energy and Morawetz estimate] for all
k\in\{0,\ldots,k_{0}-k^{\prime}\}
,
p\in[p_{1},p_{2}]
, and
\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}>\tau_{1}^{\prime}\geq\tau_{0}
,

\displaystyle F(k,p,\tau_{2})+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}F(k-k^{\prime},p-1,\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim F(k+k^{\prime},p,\tau_{1})+\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{p-p_{2}}D(k+k^{\prime},p,\tau_{1}^{\prime}),

(2.43d)

then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
k\in\{0,\ldots,k_{0}-Ck^{\prime}\}
, all
p\in[p_{1},p_{2}]
, and all
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
,

\displaystyle F(k,p,\tau_{2})\lesssim_{p_{2},p_{1}}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}\rangle^{p-p_{2}}(F(k+Ck^{\prime},p_{2},\tau_{1})+D(k+Ck^{\prime},p_{2},\tau_{1})).

(2.44)

3.
System of equations

In this section, we derive various systems of equations from the Teukolsky master equation (TME) satisfied by the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components. The TME is introduced in Section
3.1
. Then we derive in Section
3.2
coupled wave systems for each of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components, followed by a derivation of the wave equations for the modes in Section
3.3
. In the end, we discuss the Teukolsky–Starobinsky identities (TSI) in Section
3.4
.

3.1.
Teukolsky master equation

We introduce a few scalars defined from the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components.

Definition 3.1
.

Define two rescaled spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components

\displaystyle\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathfrak{s}}{\Upsilon}_{+\mathfrak{s}},

\displaystyle\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\Sigma^{-\mathfrak{s}}(r-ia\cos\theta)^{2\mathfrak{s}}{\Upsilon}_{-\mathfrak{s}}.

(3.1)

Define their radiation field

\displaystyle\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}.

(3.2)

It is a remarkable discovery by Teukolsky
[
91
]
that the scalars
\psi_{s}
in a Kerr spacetime satisfy the celebrated

Teukolsky Master Equation
(TME), a separable, decoupled wave equation.

Proposition 3.2

(TME of the spin
s
components)
.

In a Kerr spacetime, the scalars
\psi_{s}
solve the following TME in the Boyer–Lindquist coordinates:

\displaystyle 0=\mathbb{T}_{s}\psi_{s}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-a^{2}\sin^{2}{\theta}\Delta}{\Delta}\partial_{tt}^{2}\psi_{s}+\partial_{r}(\Delta\partial_{r}\psi_{s})-\frac{4aMr}{\Delta}\partial_{t\phi}^{2}\psi_{s}-\frac{a^{2}}{\Delta}\partial_{\phi\phi}^{2}\psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sin{\theta}}\partial_{\theta}(\sin{\theta}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{s})+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}{\theta}}\partial_{\phi\phi}^{2}\psi_{s}+\frac{2is\cos{\theta}}{\sin^{2}{\theta}}\partial_{\phi}\psi_{s}-(s^{2}\cot^{2}{\theta}+s)\psi_{s}

\displaystyle-2ias\cos{\theta}\partial_{t}\psi_{s}+2s[(r-M)Y-2r\partial_{t}]\psi_{s}.

(3.3)

We remark that these N–P scalars satisfying TME differ from the ones used in
[
91
]
by a rescaling factor of
2^{-s/2}\Delta^{s}
, and the reason that we use these scalars lies in the fact that both of they are regular at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
from formula (
1.4
). Note that the second line of (
3.2
) equals

\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\psi_{s}
, and this makes the TME a spin-weighted wave equation in the sense that the TME operator
\mathbb{T}_{s}
is a second-order spin-weighted operator. It serves as a starting model for quite many results in obtaining quantitative estimates for these fields, including the scalar field, the Maxwell field and the linearized gravity.

We define a (spin-weighted) wave operator that is different from the TME operator
\mathbb{T}_{s}
and useful in deriving the wave equations for the radiation fields.

Definition 3.3
.

Define a spin-weighted wave operator

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\doteq{}

\displaystyle-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV+\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}-2ias\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}.

The two wave operators
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
and
\mathbb{T}_{s}
can be related via the following statement.

Lemma 3.4
.

For any spin
s
scalar
\varphi
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\varphi)={}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\bigg(\mathbb{T}_{s}-2s[(r-M)Y-2r\partial_{t}]-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}+\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\varphi.

Proof.

We calculate in the Boyer–Lindquist coordinates that

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt(r^{2}+a^{2})YV(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\varphi)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}(\partial_{t}+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}-\mu\partial_{r})(\partial_{t}+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}+\mu\partial_{r})(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\varphi)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}(\partial_{t}+\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}-\mu\partial_{r})\big(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{a}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\partial_{\phi}+\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\partial_{r}+\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\big)\varphi.

By expanding this formula, one finds

\displaystyle-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\varphi)={}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\bigg(-\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta}\partial_{tt}^{2}\varphi+\partial_{r}(\Delta\partial_{r}\varphi)-\frac{2a(r^{2}+a^{2})}{\Delta}\partial_{t\phi}^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle-\frac{a^{2}}{\Delta}\partial_{\phi\phi}^{2}\varphi-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\phi}\varphi+\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\varphi\bigg).

(3.20)

In view of the definitions of the TME operator
\mathbb{T}_{s}
in (
3.2
) and
the wave operator
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
in (
3.3
), the claim then follows.
∎

Corollary 3.5

(TME for radiation fields of the spin
s
components)
.

The radiation field scalars
\Psi_{s}
then satisfy the following wave equation that we call as TME as well:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Psi_{s}={}

\displaystyle-2s((r-M)Y-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Psi_{s}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Psi_{s}.

(3.29)

3.1.1.
Alternative form of TME in hyperboloidal coordinates

We recast the TME under the hyperboloidal coordinates.

Proposition 3.6
.

The scalars
\psi_{s}
satisfy the following wave equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{-s+1}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s})+2a\Delta^{-s}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}+\Delta^{-s}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\psi_{s}=\Delta^{-s}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}H[\psi_{s}]

(3.30)

with

\displaystyle H[\psi_{s}]={}

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\Big(2(r^{2}+a^{2})(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-1)\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{s})

\displaystyle+\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\big[a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta+(r^{2}+a^{2})H_{\text{hyp}}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\big]\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{s}+2a\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\big[1+\mu^{-1}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\big]\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\big[(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})+2s((r-M)(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})-2r)-2ias\cos\theta\big]\psi_{s}\Big).

(3.31)

Proof.

We substitute in the formula (
2.17
) to deduce

\displaystyle-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV\varphi={}

\displaystyle-(r^{2}+a^{2})(-\partial_{\rho}+(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\bigg(\mu\partial_{\rho}+\mu H_{\text{hyp}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}+\frac{2a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\bigg)\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\mu\partial_{\rho})\varphi+2(r^{2}+a^{2})(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\partial_{\rho}\varphi+(r^{2}+a^{2})H_{\text{hyp}}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle+2a\mu^{-1}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\varphi+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\varphi-\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\varphi+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\varphi

and

\displaystyle 2s((r-M)Y-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Psi_{s}={}

\displaystyle 2s((r-M)(-\partial_{\rho}+(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi})-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+2s((r-M)(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})-2r)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{s}.

Then by the TME (
3.5
) of
\Psi_{s}
and the definition of the wave operator
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
in (
3.3
), we obtain the following wave equation in the hyperboloidal coordinates for
\Psi_{s}
:

\displaystyle 0={}

\displaystyle\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\Psi_{s}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}\Psi_{s}-2ias\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\mu\partial_{\rho})\Psi_{s}+2(r^{2}+a^{2})(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+(r^{2}+a^{2})H_{\text{hyp}}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+2a\mu^{-1}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{s}-\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+2s((r-M)(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})-2r)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{s}+\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\mu\partial_{\rho})\Psi_{s}-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\Psi_{s}+\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Psi_{s}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}H[\Psi_{s}]

(3.32)

with

\displaystyle\hat{H}[\Psi_{s}]={}

\displaystyle 2(r^{2}+a^{2})(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-1)\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+(a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta+(r^{2}+a^{2})H_{\text{hyp}}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2))\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{s}+2a(1+\mu^{-1}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-2))\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\big((r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})+2s((r-M)(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})-2r)-2ias\cos\theta\big)\Psi_{s}.

Hence, with the definition
\psi_{s}=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi_{s}
, one finds

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\mu\partial_{\rho})\Psi_{s}-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\Psi_{s}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\mu\partial_{\rho})(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{s})-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{s})+2a\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mu\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s})+\mu\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{r}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}})\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}-2s(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}\Big)

\displaystyle+\big({r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{r}(\mu\partial_{r}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}))-2s(r-M)\partial_{r}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}})\big)\psi_{s}+2a\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\big(\Delta^{s}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{-s+1}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s})\big)-\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Psi_{s}+2a\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{s}.

Plugging this back into equation (
3.1.1
) yields equation (
3.30
) for
\psi_{s}
.
∎

In addition, for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component, we have

Corollary 3.7
.

Let

\displaystyle\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\doteq\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}.

(3.33)

It then satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+2\mathfrak{s})\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}=\mathcal{L}_{\xi}H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}]

(3.34)

with
H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}]
defined as in equation (
3.6
).

Proof.

With the definition (
3.6
), we substitute
\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}
into (
3.30
) with
s=+\mathfrak{s}
and find that the LHS equals

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}))+2a\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}+2\mathfrak{s}(r-M)\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+2a\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+2\mathfrak{s}(r-M)\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}+2a\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+2\mathfrak{s})\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\big(\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+2\mathfrak{s})\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\big).

This thus yields equation (
3.34
).
∎

Remark 3.8
.

The main reason that we derive equation (
3.30
) (actually mainly for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component) and equation (
3.34
) for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component is that when projecting both equations on the
\mathfrak{s}
mode, the terms
\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}
and
\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+2\mathfrak{s})(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}
vanish due to (
2.29
). This property is essential in the analysis in Sections
4.5
and
5
.

3.2.
Wave systems for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components

In this subsection, we define a few scalars constructed from the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components and derive their governing equations. These equations are crucial in deriving the energy decay estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components.

We begin with a definition of these scalars.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
and define for the spin
s
components the following spin
s
scalars

\displaystyle\Phi_{s}^{(0)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\mu^{-s}\Psi_{s},\qquad\Phi_{s}^{(i)}\doteq{}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}.

(3.35)

Define additionally the following spin
+\mathfrak{s}
scalars

\displaystyle\Xi^{(0)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\mathfrak{s}}\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}},\qquad\Xi^{(i)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\doteq{}(-(r^{2}+a^{2})Y)^{i}\Xi^{(0)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}.

(3.36)

To derive the governing equations of the above defined scalars, we calculate the commutators between the wave operator
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}
and some other operators.

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
\varphi
be a spin
s
scalar.

•

For any function
f=f(r)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}(f\varphi)=f\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\varphi+2\Delta\partial_{r}f\partial_{r}\varphi+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu\partial_{r}f)\varphi.

•

The commutator between
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
and
\hat{\mathcal{V}}
is

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s},\hat{\mathcal{V}}]\varphi={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\varphi-\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi-\frac{2(r^{4}-6Mr^{3}+10a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi.

Proof.

Formula (
•
‣
3.10
) can be directly verified.

By formula (
3.3
) and the commutator relations in Proposition
2.6
,

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s},\hat{\mathcal{V}}]\varphi={}

\displaystyle[-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV,\hat{\mathcal{V}}]\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}((r^{2}+a^{2})YV\varphi)-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\mu Y\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi)-\hat{\mathcal{V}}\bigg(\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)(r^{2}+a^{2})\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi\bigg)-\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\mu Y\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi)-\mu^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})[\mu Y,V]\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi

\displaystyle-\mu^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})[\mu Y,V]\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\varphi-\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi

\displaystyle+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\hat{\mathcal{V}}\varphi.

(3.69)

Calculating the coefficient of the last term then yields (
•
‣
3.10
).
∎

The following two propositions then provide the governing equations of the scalars
\Phi_{s}^{(i)}
.

Proposition 3.11
.

The scalar
\Phi_{s}^{(0)}
defined above satisfies a wave equation

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}-\frac{2(2s+1)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\bigg(2s-\frac{(2s+1)(2(s+1)Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-2(s+2)a^{2}Mr+a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(0)}.

(3.78)

Proof.

Since
\Psi_{s}
satisfies the TME (
3.5
), we obtain by taking
f=\mu^{-s}
in (
•
‣
3.10
) that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle\mu^{-s}\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Psi_{s}+2\Delta\partial_{r}(\mu^{-s})\partial_{r}\Psi_{s}+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu\partial_{r}(\mu^{-s}))\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\big(-2s\mu^{-s}((r-M)Y-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})-2ar(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1}\mu^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}+2\Delta\partial_{r}(\mu^{-s})\partial_{r}\big)\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle+\bigg((r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu\partial_{r}(\mu^{-s}))-\mu^{-s}\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\Psi_{s}.

(3.95)

The second line equals

\displaystyle 2s\mu^{-s}\bigg(\frac{r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\hat{V}\Psi_{s}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}\bigg)-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mu^{-\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{s}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\hat{V}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}-\frac{(4s+2)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}+\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)\partial_{r}(\mu^{s})}{\mu^{s}(r^{2}+a^{2})}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}.

Putting this into (
3.11
) and substituting in
\Psi_{s}=\mu^{s}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}
, we find that the coefficient of the
\Phi_{s}^{(0)}
term on the RHS of (
3.11
) is equal to

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+s(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1+s}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{\partial_{r}\mu}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{s}}\bigg)\bigg)

which further equals the coefficient of the
\Phi_{s}^{(0)}
term in equation (
3.11
).
Thus, we achieve (
3.11
).
∎

Proposition 3.12
.

The scalars
\Phi_{s}^{(i)}
defined in Definition
3.9
satisfy the following wave equations

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(i)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(s+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(i)}+\sum_{{0\leq j\leq i-1,\frac{i-j-1}{2}\in\mathbb{N}}}X_{s,i,j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-(2s+i)(i+1)\Phi_{s}^{(i)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}{Z_{s,i,j}}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}+\sum_{n=0,1}\sum_{j=0}^{i}w_{s,i,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{s}^{(j)},

(3.104)

with functions
w_{s,i,j,n}=O(r^{-1})
.
Here,
Z_{s,i,j}
are constants which can be calculated as in the proof and the constants
X_{s,i,j}
are

\begin{split}X_{s,i,j}={}&(-1)^{\frac{i-j-1}{2}}(2a)^{i-j}((2s+1)C_{i}^{j}+2C_{i}^{j-1}),\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N},1\leq j\leq i,\\
X_{s,i,0}={}&(-1)^{\frac{i-1}{2}}(2a)^{i}(2s+1),\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\,\qquad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}\end{split}

(3.105)

with
C_{i}^{j}=\frac{i!}{j!(i-j)!}
.

Proof.

Applying once
\hat{\mathcal{V}}
on both sides of the wave equation (
3.11
) and using the commutator formula (
•
‣
3.10
), the LHS equals

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}+\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}+\frac{2(r^{4}-6Mr^{3}+10a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{s}^{(1)},

and the RHS equals

\displaystyle\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(2s+1)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}-(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{2(2s+1)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\bigg(2s-\frac{(2s+1)(2(s+1)Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-2(s+2)a^{2}Mr+a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{(2s+1)(2(s+1)Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-2(s+2)a^{2}Mr+a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{2s(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}-\frac{2(2s+1)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}+\frac{2(2s+1)a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\bigg(2s-\frac{(2s+1)(2(s+1)Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-2(s+2)a^{2}Mr+a^{4})-2s(r^{4}-6Mr^{3}+10a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(2s+1)((s+1)Mr^{4}+a^{2}r^{3}-(6s+9)a^{2}Mr^{2}+a^{4}r+(s+2)a^{4}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(s+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}-\frac{2(2s+3)ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(1)}+\frac{2(2s+1)a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\bigg(2(2s+1)-\frac{2(s+1)(2s+7)Mr^{3}+(6s+5)a^{2}r^{2}-2(2s^{2}+15s+12)a^{2}Mr+(6s+5)a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(2s+1)((s+1)Mr^{4}+a^{2}r^{3}-(6s+9)a^{2}Mr^{2}+a^{4}r+(s+2)a^{4}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{s}^{(0)}.

(3.114)

Applying further the operator
\hat{\mathcal{V}}
on both sides of (
3.2
) and repeated application of the commutator formula (
•
‣
3.10
) yields that the scalars
\Phi_{s}^{(i)}

(i\geq 0)
satisfy the following equation

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\Phi_{s}^{(i)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(s+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{s}^{(i)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{0\leq j\leq i,\frac{i-j}{2}\in\mathbb{N}}(-1)^{\frac{i-j}{2}}(2a)^{i-j}\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}+\sum_{0\leq j\leq i,\frac{i-j-1}{2}\in\mathbb{N}}(-1)^{\frac{i-j-1}{2}}(2a)^{i-j}\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}\frac{(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-\bigg((2s+i)(i+1)+\frac{W_{s,i,i}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{s}^{(i)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\frac{W_{s,i,j}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}

(3.123)

with the following iterative relations for the appeared constants and functions:
the constants
\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}
obey

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i,i}={}

\displaystyle 2s+2i+1,\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall i\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}={}

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i-1,j-1}+\tilde{X}_{s,i-1,j},\quad\,\,\,\,\forall 1\leq j\leq i-1,

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i,0}={}

\displaystyle 2s+1,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}

and the functions
W_{s,i,j}
obey

\displaystyle W_{s,i,i}={}

\displaystyle W_{s,i-1,i-1}-4(s+i)(3Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-5a^{2}Mr+a^{4}),

\displaystyle W_{s,i,j}={}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{W_{s,i-1,j}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)+W_{s,i-1,j-1}=(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}W_{s,i-1,j}-4rW_{s,i-1,j}+W_{s,i-1,j-1}

with the initial one

W_{s,0,0}=-(2s+1)(2(s+1)Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-2(s+2)a^{2}Mr+a^{4})

that can be read off from equation (
3.11
) and
W_{s,i,-1}=0
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
The above iterative relations for constants
\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}
yield that

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i,j}={}

\displaystyle(2s+1)C_{i}^{j}+2C_{i}^{j-1},\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N},1\leq j\leq i,

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{s,i,0}={}

\displaystyle 2s+1,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{N}.

Meanwhile, one can compute the functions
W_{s,i,j}
from the above iterative relations.
By defining the coefficient of
r^{4}
term in each
W_{s,i,j}
as the value of
Z_{s,i,j}
and isolating the constant part of the coefficients in the second line of equation (
3.2
), the claim then follows.
∎

The above also yields equations for
\{\Xi^{(i)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\}_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}}
. The wave systems for the scalars
\{\Phi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}\}_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}}
and
\{\Xi^{(i)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\}_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}}
are derived below, and the importance of these systems are crucial in obtaining the basic energy and Morawetz estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components in Kerr spacetime
[
68
,
69
]
. The following equations for the radiation fields in
\mathfrak{s}=1
and
\mathfrak{s}=2
cases are also derived in
[
67
,
3
]
respectively.

Corollary 3.13
.

We have the following basic wave systems for the scalars
\{\Phi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}\}_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}}
and
\{\Xi^{(i)}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\}_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}}
defined in Definition
3.9
:

•

for
s=0
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{0}\Phi^{(0)}_{0}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi^{(0)}_{0}+\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi^{(0)}_{0};

•

for
s=-1
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-1}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}+\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}+\bigg(2-\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-1}^{(0)},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-1}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle\bigg(2-\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}-\frac{2a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}+\frac{2a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-1}\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}-\frac{6ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}+\frac{12Mr^{3}+3a^{2}r^{2}-18a^{2}Mr+3a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}-\frac{8a^{3}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}-\frac{2a^{2}(r^{4}-6Mr^{3}+10a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)};

(3.141)

•

for
s=+1
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+1}\Xi_{+1}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+1}^{(1)}-\frac{6ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+1}^{(0)}+\bigg(2-\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Xi_{+1}^{(0)},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+1}\Xi_{+1}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle\bigg(2-\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Xi_{+1}^{(1)}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+1}^{(1)}+\frac{2a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+1}^{(0)}+\frac{2a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+1}^{(0)};

•

for spin
-2
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-2}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{4(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}+\frac{6ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+\bigg(4+\frac{6Mr^{3}-3a^{2}r^{2}-3a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-2}^{(0)},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-2}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}+\bigg(6-\frac{6Mr^{3}+7a^{2}r^{2}-20a^{2}Mr+7a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}-\frac{6a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}-\frac{6Mr^{4}-6a^{2}r^{3}-18a^{2}Mr^{2}-6a^{4}r}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)},

(3.143q)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-2}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}={}

\displaystyle\bigg(6-\frac{6Mr^{3}+7a^{2}r^{2}-20a^{2}Mr+7a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{20a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}-\frac{8a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{24a^{3}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}-\frac{6a^{2}(r^{4}+10Mr^{3}-6a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

(3.143z)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-2}\Phi_{-2}^{(3)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{-2}^{(3)}-\frac{6ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(3)}+\bigg(4+\frac{6Mr^{3}-3a^{2}r^{2}-3a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Phi_{-2}^{(3)}

\displaystyle-\frac{6a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}+\frac{6Mr^{4}+34a^{2}r^{3}-138a^{2}Mr^{2}+34a^{4}r+40a^{4}M}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle-\frac{56a^{3}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}-\frac{26a^{2}r^{4}-60a^{2}Mr^{3}+164a^{4}Mr-26a^{6}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{24a^{3}(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+\frac{60a^{2}Mr^{4}-24a^{4}r^{3}-288a^{4}Mr^{2}-24a^{6}r+36a^{6}M}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)},

(3.144i)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-2}\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{4(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}+\frac{10ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}+\frac{5(6Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-8a^{2}Mr+a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}

\displaystyle+\frac{40a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(3)}

\displaystyle+\frac{80a^{3}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}-\frac{60a^{2}(r^{4}-6Mr^{3}+10a^{2}Mr+a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{80a^{3}(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}-\frac{128a^{4}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{96a^{5}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+\frac{24a^{4}(r^{4}+34Mr^{3}-30a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)};

(3.144r)

•

for
s=+2
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+2}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{4(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}-\frac{10ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)}+\bigg(4+\frac{6Mr^{3}-3a^{2}r^{2}-3a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Xi_{+2}^{(0)},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+2}\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+2}^{(2)}+\bigg(6-\frac{6Mr^{3}+7a^{2}r^{2}-20a^{2}Mr+7a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{6ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}+\frac{6a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)}-\frac{6Mr^{4}-6a^{2}r^{3}-18a^{2}Mr^{2}-6a^{4}r}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)},

(3.145q)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+2}\Xi_{+2}^{(2)}={}

\displaystyle\bigg(6-\frac{6Mr^{3}+7a^{2}r^{2}-20a^{2}Mr+7a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\Xi_{+2}^{(2)}-\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{20a^{2}(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}-\frac{8a(r^{2}-a^{2})}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{24a^{3}r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)}-\frac{6a^{2}(r^{4}+10Mr^{3}-6a^{2}Mr-a^{4})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Xi_{+2}^{(0)}.

(3.145z)

For the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, it is surprising that a linear combination of
\{\Phi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i_{0})}\}_{i_{0}\leq i}
satisfies the basically the same equation as the one of
\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i-2\mathfrak{s})}
, for any
i\geq 2\mathfrak{s}
. This allows us to focus on one single spin component when deriving the energy decay estimates as the argument for the other spin component is similar. Cf. Section
4
. Such a linear combination is as follows.

Definition 3.14
.

•

For
\mathfrak{s}=0
, define
\dot{\Phi}_{0}^{(i)}\doteq\Phi_{0}^{(i)}
for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
;

•

For
\mathfrak{s}=1
, define

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{-1}^{(2)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}+a^{2}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)},

(3.146a)

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{-1}^{(i)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i-2}\dot{\Phi}_{-1}^{(2)},\quad\forall i>2;

(3.146b)

•

For
\mathfrak{s}=2
, define

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{-2}^{(4)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}+10a^{2}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}+9a^{4}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)},

(3.147a)

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{-2}^{(i)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i-4}\dot{\Phi}_{-2}^{(4)},\quad\forall i>4.

(3.147b)

We can derive the governing equations for the above-defined scalars
\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}
for
i\geq 2\mathfrak{s}
.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}\in\{0,1,2\}
and let
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
The scalars
\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}
satisfy the following wave equations

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{-\mathfrak{s}}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(\mathfrak{s}+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq i-1,\\
\frac{i-j-1}{2}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}X_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+j)}

\displaystyle-i(2\mathfrak{s}+i+1)\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+j)}+\sum_{n=0,1}\sum_{j=0}^{i}w_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+j)},

(3.156)

with functions
w_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}
and constants

Z_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}
and
X_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}
being the same as in Proposition
3.12
.

Proof.

First, equations of
\dot{\Phi}_{-1}^{(2)}
and
\dot{\Phi}_{-2}^{(4)}
can be verified directly from Definition
3.14
and using the equations in Corollary
3.13
. This proves
i=0
case.

Then, one notices that the RHS of the governing equation of
\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s})}
is in the same form as the one of equation (
3.12
) for
i=0
and
s=+\mathfrak{s}
. (Note that however the constant coefficient of
\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s})}
term on the RHS differs from the one of
\Phi_{+2}^{(0)}
term on the RHS of equation (
3.12
) for
i=0
and
s=+\mathfrak{s}
.) Equation (
3.15
) for general
i>0
can then be proven in an exactly same manner as proving equation (
3.12
) in the proof of Proposition
3.12
.
∎

We then define new scalars
\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}
(resp.
\hat{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}
) constructed from a linear combination (with constant coefficients) of
\{\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i^{\prime})}\}_{i^{\prime}\leq i}
(resp.
\{\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i^{\prime}+2\mathfrak{s})}\}_{i^{\prime}\leq i}
) such that we can eliminate the term
-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}{Z_{s,i,j}}\Phi_{s}^{(j)}
in equation (
3.12
) (resp. the term
-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+j)}
in equation (
3.15
)). These eliminated terms are obstructions to deriving
r^{p}
estimates for an extended range of
p
, thus to deriving further energy decay estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components. It is these linear combinations that successfully remove these terms and these combinations are unique
5
5

5

The uniqueness can be seen from the proof.
up to an overall nonzero multiplicative constant.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. There exist constants
\{x_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}\}_{0\leq j\leq i-1,0\leq n\leq i-j}
such that the scalars
\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}
defined by

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}+\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\sum_{n=0}^{i-j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}

(3.157a)

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}\doteq{}

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}+\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\sum_{n=0}^{i-j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+j)}

(3.157b)

satisfy the following wave equations

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(s+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}-(i+2s)(i+1)\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}+\hat{H}_{s,i}

with

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq i}\sum_{n\leq d_{i}}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)},

(3.166a)

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{-\mathfrak{s},i-2\mathfrak{s}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq i}\sum_{n\leq d_{i}}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}

(3.166b)

where the coefficient of the term
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}
is the same as the coefficient of the term
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}
in the above formulas (
3.166
)
and
d_{i}=i+1
.
6
6

6

The proof actually shows that one can take
d_{i}=i+1-j
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
s=+\mathfrak{s}
case, since the proof for
s=-\mathfrak{s}
case is exactly the same in view of the fact that equation (
3.15
) of
\dot{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)}
is in a same form as equation (
3.12
) of
\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}
for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

To illustrate better the idea of this proof, we define the constants
V_{\mathfrak{s},i}=2(\mathfrak{s}+i)
and
Y_{\mathfrak{s},i}=(2\mathfrak{s}+i)(i+1)
and denote the last two terms in (
3.12
) as
H_{+\mathfrak{s},i}
, that is,
H_{+\mathfrak{s},i}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}O(r^{-1})\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}
. Equation (
3.12
) can then be written as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}V_{\mathfrak{s},i}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}-Y_{\mathfrak{s},i}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq i-1,\\
\frac{i-j-1}{2}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}{X}_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i,j}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+H_{+\mathfrak{s},i}.

We shall prove the statement by induction.
In view of equation (
3.11
),
\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}=\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}
clearly satisfies (
3.16
) with
i=0
. We then proceed by
assuming that we have chosen the constants
\{x_{\mathfrak{s},i,j,n}\}_{0\leq j\leq i-1,0\leq n\leq i-j}
such that
\{\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}\}_{0\leq j\leq i}
satisfy (
3.16
), that is,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}V_{\mathfrak{s},j}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-Y_{\mathfrak{s},j}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},j}.

Using the general ansatz (
3.157
),
the above two equations then yield that
\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i+1)}
satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+\mathfrak{s}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i+1)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}V_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i+1)}

\displaystyle-Y_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i+1)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq i,\\
\frac{i-j}{2}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}{X}_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}Y_{\mathfrak{s},j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+\frac{(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}(V_{\mathfrak{s},j}-V_{\mathfrak{s},i+1})x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i+1}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},j}.

(3.175)

The remaining step is to choose the constants
x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}
such that the second line of the above equation equal
-Y_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i+1)}
. This is equivalent to requiring

\displaystyle Y_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq i,\\
\frac{i-j}{2}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}{X}_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}Y_{\mathfrak{s},j}x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}=0.

By substituting in
\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}=\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum\limits_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}\sum\limits_{n=0}^{j-j^{\prime}}x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j^{\prime})}
that comes from (
3.157
), the above equation becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}(Y_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}-Y_{\mathfrak{s},j})x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}}\bigg(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum\limits_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}\sum\limits_{n=0}^{j-j^{\prime}}x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j^{\prime})}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq i,\\
\frac{i-j}{2}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}{X}_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}\bigg(\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\sum\limits_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}\sum\limits_{n=0}^{j-j^{\prime}}x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n+1}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j^{\prime})}\bigg).

(3.176)

Since the values of the constants
\{X_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}\}_{0\leq j\leq i}
and
\{Z_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j}\}_{0\leq j\leq i}
are given in Proposition
3.12
and the difference
Y_{\mathfrak{s},i+1}-Y_{\mathfrak{s},j}=(i-j+1)(i+j+2\mathfrak{s}+2)
is non-zero for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and since the values of constants
\{x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}\}_{0\leq j\leq i,0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j-1,0\leq n\leq j-j^{\prime}}
are given, there is a unique solution for
\{x_{\mathfrak{s},i+1,j,n}\}_{0\leq j\leq i,0\leq n\leq i+1-j}
to equation (
3.2
).

Finally, we denote the last two lines of (
3.2
) as
\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i+1}
. We shall show that

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq i}\sum_{n\leq i+1-j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}.

(3.177)

which clearly yields (
3.166a
).
Note from (
3.157
) that

\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i-j}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}
, and by substituting this into the second last line of (
3.2
), one finds this second last line equals

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}\bigg(\sum_{x=0}^{j}\sum_{y=0}^{j-x}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{y}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(x)}\bigg)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j+1)}.

By the induction assumption (
3.177
) for
\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i}
, the last term in (
3.2
) equals

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+1-j}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\bigg(\sum_{x\leq j+1-y}\sum_{y=0}^{j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{x}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(y)}\bigg)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n=0}^{i+2-j}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}.

In view of these discussions, we therefore conclude
(
3.177
) for
\hat{H}_{+\mathfrak{s},i+1}
and prove (
3.177
) for general
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

3.3.
Wave equations for the modes of spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components

The following definition is useful to calculate the commutator between the wave operator
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
and mode projection operators.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
\varphi_{s}
be a spin
s
scalar. Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]\doteq{}

\displaystyle-a^{2}[\mathbf{P}_{\ell}^{s},\sin^{2}\theta](\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\varphi_{s})+2ias[\mathbf{P}_{\ell}^{s},\cos\theta](\varphi_{s}),

(3.178a)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{m,\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]\doteq{}

\displaystyle-a^{2}[\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{s},\sin^{2}\theta](\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\varphi_{s})+2ias[\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{s},\cos\theta](\varphi_{s}),

(3.178b)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\geq\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]\doteq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\geq\ell}\mathbf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{s}[\varphi_{s}].

(3.178c)

It holds

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\geq\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]+\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell-1}\mathbf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]=0

(3.179)

and

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s},\mathbf{P}_{\ell}^{s}]\varphi_{s}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}],

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s},\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{s}]\varphi_{s}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{m,\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}],

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s},\mathbf{P}_{\geq\ell}^{s}]\varphi_{s}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\geq\ell}^{s}[\varphi_{s}]=-\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell-1}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{s}[\varphi_{s}].

By projecting (
3.16
) onto an
\ell
mode and using the above definition, we achieve

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
\ell\geq\mathfrak{s}
, and let
\mathfrak{s}-s\leq i\leq\ell-s
.
The scalars
(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}
, the
\ell
mode of
\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}
that is defined in (
3.157
),
satisfy the following wave equations

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(s+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}-(2s+i)(i+1)(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+(\hat{H}_{s,i})_{\ell}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{s}[\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}],

with
(\hat{H}_{s,i})_{\ell}
being the
\ell
mode of
\hat{H}_{s,i}
defined in (
3.166
).

Further, we base on the above result and define a new scalar supported on a fixed mode such that it satisfies a transport equation with the source enjoying faster decay in
r
, a property that is essential in further extending the
r^{p}
hierarchy in order to achieve almost sharp decay in Section
4.4
.

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
\ell\geq\mathfrak{s}
, and let
i\in\mathbb{N}
. The scalars
\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}
defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}\doteq\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-2ias\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}\big)\Big)

(3.189)

satisfy the following wave equations

\displaystyle-\mu Y\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}-\frac{2(\ell+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}={}

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{s,\ell},

(3.190)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{s,\ell}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\leq d_{\ell-s}}\sum_{\mathfrak{s}-s\leq j\leq\ell-s}O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{n}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(j)})_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0,1}O(r^{-1})(rV)^{j}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-2ias\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}\big)

\displaystyle+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-2ias\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}\big)

(3.191)

and
d_{\ell-s}
a constant depending only on
\ell-s
.

Further, by defining
\tilde{\Phi}_{s,m,\ell}
and
\tilde{H}_{s,m,\ell}
as the
m
azimuthal modes of
\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}
and
\tilde{H}_{s,\ell}
respectively, it satisfies

\displaystyle-\mu Y\tilde{\Phi}_{s,m,\ell}-\frac{2(\ell+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{s,m,\ell}={}

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{s,m,\ell}.

(3.192)

Remark 3.20
.

The scalar
\tilde{\Phi}_{s,m,\ell}
actually equals the Newman–Penrose constant of the
(m,\ell)
mode of the spin
s
component in the nonvanishing N–P constant case in
[
10
,
67
,
11
,
71
]
.

Proof.

We have shown in the above proposition that projecting (
3.16
) onto an
\ell
mode leads to equation (
3.18
), which can be expanded into

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt-(r^{2}+a^{2})YV(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}))-2ias\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)}))

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+(2s+i)(i+1))(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+\frac{2(s+i)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+(\hat{H}_{s,i})_{\ell}.

(3.193)

Substituting in
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}=\frac{1}{2}(\mu Y+V)-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
, the LHS of equation (
3.3
) equals

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt-\mu Y\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})-2ias\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})\big)-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\mu Y\bigg(\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})-2ias\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})\big)\bigg)-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}V-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\bigg)\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})-2ias\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})\big).

From now on, take
i=\ell-s
. Then by (
2.29
),

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}+(2s+i)(i+1))(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(i)})_{\ell}=(-(\ell+s)(\ell-s+1)+(\ell+s)(\ell-s+1))(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}=0.

The above discussions together thus yield that the scalar
\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}
defined in (
3.189
) satisfies

\displaystyle-\mu Y\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2(\ell+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}

\displaystyle+(\hat{H}_{s,\ell-s})_{\ell}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}V-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\bigg)\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})-2ias\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})\big).

(3.194)

We use (
3.189
) to rewrite
\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}
as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}=\tilde{\Phi}_{s,\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-2ias\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}\big)

and substitute this into equation (
3.3
), then
the desired equation (
3.190
) holds with

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{s,\ell}={}

\displaystyle(\hat{H}_{s,\ell-s})_{\ell}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}V-\frac{a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\bigg)\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})_{\ell}+a^{2}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})-2ias\mathbf{P}_{\ell}(\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)})\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{(\ell+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\ell}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}-2ias\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{s}^{(\ell-s)}\big).

(3.195)

This expression can manifestly be put into the form of (
3.19
).
∎

3.4.
Teukolsky–Starobinsky identities

As we have discussed, the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components are in fact related to each other by purely differential relations–the
Teukolsky-Starobinsky identities
(TSI)
[
92
,
85
]
. The covariant form of these identities is derived in
[
1
]
. These identities are of fundamental importance in our analysis for both of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components in this paper.

Lemma 3.21
.

(1)

There are the following TSI for the spin
\pm 1
components

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{2}\psi_{+1}={}

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{V}^{2}(\Delta\psi_{-1}),

(3.196a)

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}+ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{2}\psi_{-1}={}

\displaystyle Y^{2}\psi_{+1}.

(3.196b)

Further, equation (
3.196a
) can be written as

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{2}\Phi_{+1}^{(0)}={}\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}+a^{2}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}=\dot{\Phi}_{-1}^{(2)}.

(3.197)

(2)

There are the following TSI for the spin
\pm 2
components of the linearized gravity:

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{4}\psi_{+2}-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{+2}}={}

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}\hat{V}^{4}(\Delta^{2}\psi_{-2}),

(3.198a)

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}+ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{4}\psi_{-2}+12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{-2}}={}

\displaystyle Y^{4}(\psi_{+2}).

(3.198b)

Further, equation (
3.198a
) can be written as

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{4}\Phi_{+2}^{(0)}-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+2}^{(0)}}={}

\displaystyle\Phi_{-2}^{(4)}+10a^{2}\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}+9a^{4}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}=\dot{\Phi}_{-2}^{(4)}.

(3.199)

Remark 3.22
.

We remark that these TSI will be projected on spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes and, because of the spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes coupling, the obtained equations are different from the original TSI in
[
92
]
in which a projection on spin-weighted spheroidal harmonic modes is applied and no mode coupling is present.

Proof.

The TSI (
3.196
) and (
3.198
) can be derived from the covariant form
[
1
]
, or, following the same way as in
[
92
,
85
]
. In particular, one notes that these equations (
3.196a
), (
3.196a
), (
3.198a
) and (
3.198b
) are the physical space version of equations (3.9)–(3.10), (3.15)–(3.16), (3.21)–(3.22) and (3.27)–(3.28) of
[
92
]
in the frequency space, respectively.

To show formula (
3.197
), we substitute
\Delta\psi_{-1}=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}
and
\Delta^{-1}\psi_{+1}=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-3/2}\Phi_{+1}^{(0)}
into equation (
3.196a
) and find that the RHS equals

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{V}^{2}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)})=\Delta\hat{V}\bigg(\frac{r}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}\bigg)=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}(\Phi_{-1}^{(2)}+a^{2}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}).

(3.200)

This thus proves (
3.197
). Equation (
3.199
) is similarly proven by plugging
\Delta^{2}\psi_{-2}=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}
and
\Delta^{-2}\psi_{+2}=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-5/2}\Phi_{+2}^{(0)}
into equation (
3.198a
).
∎

4.
Almost sharp decay estimates

In this section, we show the almost sharp decay for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components in a subextreme Kerr spacetime under a conditional assumption of a
basic energy and Morawetz (BEAM) estimate
(also known as
integrated local energy decay estimates
) for an inhomogeneous TME. This BEAM estimate assumption is introduced in Section
4.1
and we apply it to achieve the resulting BEAM estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components as well as for their modes in a subextreme Kerr. We then prove
r^{p}
estimates for an inhomogeneous spin-weighted wave equation and an inhomogeneous transport equation in Section
4.2
and make use of these
r^{p}
estimates together with the BEAM estimates to prove energy decay for both of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components in Section
4.3
and their modes in Section
4.4
. In the end, these energy decay estimates are utilized in Section
4.5
to prove the almost sharp decay.

4.1.
Assumptions on the BEAM estimates

To properly state the BEAM estimate assumption, we first define the energies and spacetime Morawetz integrals of spin
s
scalars.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
k\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
, let
\varsigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, and let
\delta>0
be a small constant. Let
\varphi_{s}
be an arbitrary spin
s
scalar in a subextreme Kerr spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g_{M,a})
. Let
\chi_{\text{trap}}
be a smooth real-valued function which equals
0
in the trapping region and
1
a bit away from the trapping region.
Define the following energies

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\doteq

\displaystyle{}\sum\limits_{\lvert\mathbf{a}\rvert\leq k-\mathfrak{s}-1}\bigg(\sum_{0\leq i\leq\mathfrak{s}-1}\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}(r^{-\varsigma}Y^{i}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{1}(\Sigma_{\tau})}+\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}Y^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{1}(\Sigma_{\tau})}\bigg),

(4.1)

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\doteq

\displaystyle{}\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\sum_{\lvert\mathbf{a}\rvert\leq k-\mathfrak{s}-1}\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}(r^{2}V)^{i}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{1}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}})}

(4.2)

and the following spacetime Morawetz integrals for any
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}

\displaystyle M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\doteq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\lvert\mathbf{a}\rvert\leq k-\mathfrak{s}-1}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}-1}\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}(r^{-\varsigma}Y^{i}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}+\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}Y^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}-1}\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}(r^{-\varsigma}Y^{i}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}+\lVert\chi_{\text{trap}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}(Y^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle+\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\partial_{r^{*}}(Y^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\bigg),

(4.3)

\displaystyle M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\doteq{}
