\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\sum_{\lvert\mathbf{a}\rvert\leq k-\mathfrak{s}-1}\Big(\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\mathcal{V}^{i}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}+\lVert\chi_{\text{trap}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}(\mathcal{V}^{i}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lVert\mathbb{B}^{\mathbf{a}}\partial_{r^{*}}(\mathcal{V}^{i}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3-\delta}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big).

(4.4)

We can now state our main assumption on the BEAM estimates for an inhomogeneous TME.

Assumption 4.2

(Assumption on the BEAM estimates for inhomogeneous TME)
.

Let
s\in\{0,\pm 1,\pm 2\}
. Let
M>0
and
\lvert s\rvert<M
. Let
\varphi_{s}
and
N[\varphi_{s}]
be spin
s
scalars and let
\varphi_{s}
satisfy the following inhomogeneous TME on a subextreme Kerr background:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\varphi_{s}+2s((r-M)Y-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\varphi_{s}+\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\varphi_{s}={}N[\varphi_{s}].

(4.13)

We say that the BEAM estimates assumption for this inhomogeneous TME is satisfied on a Kerr background
(\mathcal{M},g_{M,a})
if there exists
\varsigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that given any
0<\delta<1/2
and any
\mathfrak{s}+1\leq k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, there exist universal constants
k^{\prime}\geq 0
and
C=C(M,a,\delta,k)
7
7

7

This constant depends on the hyperboloidal foliation via the function
h_{\text{hyp}}=h_{\text{hyp}}(r)
. For simplicity, we shall suppress this dependence for this universal constant throughout this work as one can fix this function once for all.
such that the following
BEAM estimates are valid in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
for any
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}(\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\}}E^{k+k^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}(N[\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])+\sum_{i_{0}=0,1}\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{0}}\mathcal{V}^{i}N[\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big),

(4.14a)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\}}E^{k+k^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}(N[\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])+\sum_{i_{0}=0,1}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{0}}N[\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big).

(4.14b)

Remark 4.3
.

The requirement that we need to impose bounds over extra
k^{\prime}
-order derivatives of the inhomogeneous term is due to the well-known trapping phenomenon which causes a loss of regularity in the Morawetz estimates. In fact, as can be seen from the proof in Remark
4.4
,
k^{\prime}=1
is sufficient.

Remark 4.4
.

The BEAM estimates for the TME with vanishing inhomogeneous term are proven for
s=0
in
[
31
]
on any subextreme Kerr,
s=\pm 1
in
[
68
]
on slowly rotating Kerr and
s=\pm 2
in
[
69
]
on slowly rotating Kerr, and the proof can be easily adapted to show this BEAM estimate assumption
4.2
in these cases. Consider only
s=-\mathfrak{s}
case, the case
s=+\mathfrak{s}
being similarly treated. The general approach in these works is to consider the wave systems of
\{\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\}_{i=0,1,\ldots,2\mathfrak{s}}
(hence with inhomogeneous terms
\{\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}N[\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])\}_{i=0,1,\ldots,2\mathfrak{s}}
), therefore it suffices to bound the following integral

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{0}=0}^{k}\sum_{i=0}^{2\mathfrak{s}}\bigg|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}\Sigma^{-1}\Re\big(\partial^{k_{0}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}N[\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])\overline{X\partial^{k_{0}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})}\big)\mathrm{d}^{4}\mu\bigg|

(4.15)

by the last two terms in (
4.14
), with
X\varphi=(O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}+O(1)Y+O(r^{-1}))\varphi
. The integral outside the trapping region and the integral supported in the trapping region but arising from either the
r
-derivative part or no derivative part of
X
can all be estimated using Cauchy–Schwarz, and it remains to bound the integral of
O(1)\Sigma^{-1}\Re\big(\partial^{k_{0}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}N[\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])\overline{X\partial^{k_{0}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{i}(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi_{-\mathfrak{s}})}\big)
with
X=\mathcal{L}_{\xi},\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
in the trapping region. By an integration by parts in
X
, we then bound these integrals by the last two terms in (
4.14
), thereby proving the estimate (
4.14
).

We shall emphasis that this assumption on a subextreme Kerr background with a fixed parameter
\varsigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and a suitably large regularity parameter
k
is assumed throughout the rest of this paper.

In the case that we are considering the TME of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components with vanishing inhomogeneous term, we immediately arrive at:

Lemma 4.5

(BEAM estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components on a subextreme Kerr)
.

In the DOC of a subextreme Kerr spacetime, given any
0<\delta<1/2
and
\mathfrak{s}+1\leq k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, there exist universal constants
k^{\prime}>0
and
C=C(M,k)
such that the following
BEAM estimates are valid in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
for any
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\leq{}

\displaystyle CE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}),

(4.16a)

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\leq{}

\displaystyle CE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}).

(4.16b)

The above also hold if replacing
\Psi_{s}
by
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\Psi_{s}

(j\in\mathbb{N})
everywhere since
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}
commutes with the TME.

However, for each
\ell
mode of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components, because of the coupling with the other modes, each
\ell
mode of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components satisfies an inhomogeneous TME, and this leads to a different BEAM estimate for a fixed mode.

Lemma 4.6

(BEAM estimates for a fixed mode of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components on a subextreme Kerr)
.

Let
\ell\geq\mathfrak{s}
. In the DOC of a subextreme Kerr spacetime, given any
0<\delta<1/2
and
\mathfrak{s}+1\leq k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, there exist universal constants
k^{\prime}>0
and
C=C(M,\delta,k)
such that the following
BEAM estimates are valid in the region
\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
for any
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{V}^{i}{\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}})}+\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{V}^{i}{\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big),

(4.17a)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}{\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}})}+\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}{\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big).

(4.17b)

The above also hold if replacing
(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}
by
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}
everywhere for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Meanwhile, the above estimates hold also for
\geq\ell
modes, i.e. they are valid if we replace
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}
by
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{s})_{\geq\ell}
, respectively.

Proof.

By projecting the TME onto an
\ell
mode and in view of the expression (
3.3
)
of
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
, we achieve

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}+2s((r-M)Y-2r\mathcal{L}_{\xi})(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}

\displaystyle+\frac{2ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}+\bigg(\frac{2sr(r-M)}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr^{3}+a^{2}r^{2}-4a^{2}Mr+a^{4}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\bigg)(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}

\displaystyle={}N[(\Psi_{s})_{\ell}]=\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{s}[\Psi_{s}].

(4.26)

The assumed BEAM estimates for an inhomogeneous TME then apply and yield

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}((\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{V}^{i}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}})}+\sum_{i_{0}=0,1}\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{0}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{V}^{i}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big),

(4.27a)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217ptE^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+M^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Big(E^{k}_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}((\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\ell})+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}})}+\sum_{i_{0}=0,1}\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{0}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathbf{C}_{\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}]\rVert^{2}_{W_{-3+\delta}^{k+k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}})}\Big).

(4.27b)

In view of Definition
3.17
and Proposition
2.13
, the
desired estimates (
4.17
) then follow. The same argument applies to
\geq\ell
modes.
∎

Definition 4.7

(1)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s,G}\varphi=\vartheta

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}\varphi-b_{V}V\varphi-b_{\phi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\varphi-b_{0}\varphi=\vartheta.

\varphi

\displaystyle\mu Y_{G}\varphi=\vartheta

(4.44)

.

Lemma 4.8

(

.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathfrak{s}=\lvert s\rvert\leq 2
,
9
9

9

The statements in this lemma actually apply to general

with

.
and

.

(1)

[
r^{p}
estimate for an inhomogeneous spin-weighted wave equation].
Let
\varphi
(supported on
\geq\ell_{0}
modes) and
\vartheta
be spin
s
scalars satisfying the inhomogeneous spin-weighted wave equation (
1
).
Then there are constants
\hat{R}_{0}=\hat{R}_{0}(\ell_{0},p,k,b_{0},b_{\phi},b_{V})
and
C=C(\ell_{0},p,\hat{R}_{0},k,b_{0},b_{\phi},b_{V})
such that for all
R_{0}\geq\hat{R}_{0}
and
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
,
for
p\in(0,2)
,

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\lVert rV\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-2}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+1}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{k+1}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert Y\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{-1-\varsigma}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}{}C\Big(\lVert rV\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-2}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{-2}^{k+1}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\Big);

(4.46)

(2)

[
r^{p}
estimate for an inhomogeneous transport equation].
Assume
\varphi
and
\vartheta
be spin
s
scalars satisfying the inhomogeneous transport equation (
4.44
).
Then for any
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
, there are constants
\hat{R}_{0}=\hat{R}_{0}(\ell_{0},p,k,b_{0})
,
C_{0}=C_{0}(\ell_{0},p,\hat{R}_{0},k,b_{0})
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(\ell_{0},p,\varepsilon,k,\hat{R}_{0},b_{0})
such that for all
R_{0}\geq\hat{R}_{0}
,
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
and
p\geq\delta>0
, both of the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}{}C_{0}\Big(\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\Big);

(4.47a)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}{}C_{1}\Big(\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\tau^{1+\varepsilon}\lVert\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{k}(\Sigma_{\tau}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big).

(4.47b)

In all the above estimates, we have implicitly included in the symbol
\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}
the integral terms
\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{0}^{k+1}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{R_{0}-M,R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{0}^{k+1}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{R_{0}-M,R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{0}^{k+1}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{R_{0}-M,R_{0}})}
supported on
[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]
.

Proof.

Point (
1
) for
p\in(0,2)
has been proven in
[
3
, Lemmas 5.5 and 5.6]
. Notice that there is a sign difference between the operator
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
in this work with the one in
[
3
]
, and this also causes some sign changes in equation (
1
).

It remains to prove point (
2
). Let
\chi_{x}(r)
be a cutoff function such that it equals
1
for
r\geq x
and vanishes for
r\leq x-M
. By multiplying equation (
4.45
) by
2\chi_{R_{0}}r^{p-4}\bar{\varphi}
, taking the real part and integrating by parts, one arrives at

\displaystyle Y(\chi_{R_{0}}\mu r^{p-4}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2})+\big(\partial_{r}(\chi_{R_{0}}\mu r^{p-4})+4\chi_{R_{0}}r^{p-5}+2\chi_{R_{0}}b_{0}r^{p-4}\big)\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2}={}\Re(2\chi_{R_{0}}r^{p-4}\vartheta\bar{\varphi}).

(4.48)

The coefficient of
\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2}
term is equal to
(p+2b_{0,0})\chi_{R_{0}}r^{p-5}+r^{p-6}((p-4)\chi_{R_{0}}r(\mu-1)+r^{2}\partial_{r}(\chi_{R_{0}}\mu)+2\chi_{R_{0}}r^{2}(b_{0}-b_{0,0}r^{-1}))
, and by assumption, it is greater than
\frac{p}{2}r^{p-5}
in region
r\geq\hat{R}_{0}
for
\hat{R}_{0}
large enough. Thus, by applying a Cauchy–Schwarz to the RHS of (
4.48
) and integrating equation (
4.48
) in
\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M}
with
R_{0}\geq\hat{R}_{0}
, we obtain the estimate (
4.47
) in the case of
k=0
. On the other hand, we can also utilize the Hölder’s inequality to bound the RHS of (
4.48
) by
\varepsilon_{0}p\chi_{R_{0}}r^{p-4}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2}+\chi_{R_{]}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}p}r^{p-4}\lvert\vartheta\rvert^{2}
, then integrating over
\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M}
with
R_{0}\geq\hat{R}_{0}
yield

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau^{\prime}}^{\geq R_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}{}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\varepsilon_{0}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\tau^{1+\varepsilon}}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau}^{\geq R_{0}})}\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\tau^{1+\varepsilon}\lVert\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\mathrm{d}\tau.

By taking a supreme over
\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]
, the second term in the last line can be absorbed by the LHS, and we thus obtain the estimate (
4.47
) in the case of
k=0
.

We next commute the transport equation (
4.45
) with
r\tilde{V}
; in view of the commutator (
2.18c
), this gives

\displaystyle\mu Y(r\tilde{V}\varphi)+((b_{0}+\mu r^{-1})+2r^{-1})r\tilde{V}\varphi=r\tilde{V}\vartheta-\mu r\partial_{r}(b_{0}+2r^{-1})\varphi\doteq\vartheta_{r\tilde{V}}.

(4.49)

This equation can again be put into the form of the transport equation (
4.45
) and the assumptions are all satisfied. Thus, the estimate (
4.47
) with
k=0
holds by replacing
\varphi
and
\vartheta
by
r\tilde{V}\varphi
and
\vartheta_{r\tilde{V}}
respectively. Note that
\lVert\vartheta_{r\tilde{V}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\lesssim\lVert r\tilde{V}\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}
, and the term
\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}
is already bounded in the previous discussions. One can thus inductively show that for any
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\sum_{i_{1}\leq k}\Big(\lVert(r\tilde{V})^{i_{1}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert(r\tilde{V})^{i_{1}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}})}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{[R_{0}-M,R_{0}]}{}\sum_{{i_{1}}\leq k}\Big(\lVert(r\tilde{V})^{i_{1}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-4}^{0}(\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}^{\geq R_{0}})}+\lVert(r\tilde{V})^{i_{1}}\vartheta\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}^{\geq R_{0}-M})}\Big).

(4.50)

Since
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}

\mathring{\eth}
and
\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}
commute with the transport equation (
4.45
), the above estimate (
4.2
) manifestly hold with
\varphi
and
\vartheta
replaced by
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{i_{3}}\mathring{\eth}^{i_{4}}(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{i_{5}}\varphi
and
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{i_{3}}\mathring{\eth}^{i_{4}}(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{i_{5}}\vartheta
respectively for any
i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},i_{5}\in\mathbb{N}
. In the end, in view of the fact that the operators in the set
\mathbb{D}
can be expanded in terms of
\{r\tilde{V},\mathcal{L}_{\xi},\mathcal{L}_{\eta},\mathring{\eth},\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\}
with
O(1)
coefficients in the region
r\geq R_{0}-M
, the estimate (
4.47
) is therefore valid. The other estimate (
4.47
) for general
k\geq 0
can be proven in a completely analogous manner.
∎

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{+\mathfrak{s},G}{\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}}={}

\displaystyle 0.
