), (
4.4
) and (
4.4
) and find that the last five terms are bounded by

\displaystyle\tau_{1}^{-2+\delta+p}\big(

\displaystyle F^{(0)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),4-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})+F^{(1)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}+1})

\displaystyle+F^{(1)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+2})+F^{(0)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\big).

Hence, the same argument applies and yields for any
p\in[2+\delta,4-\delta]
,

\displaystyle\hskip-30.1388ptF^{(1)}(k,p,\tau_{2},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}+1})+F^{(0)}(k,2+\delta,\tau_{2},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})+F^{(1)}(k,\delta,\tau_{2},(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})+\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(1)})_{\mathfrak{s}+1}\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-3}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{2},\infty}^{\geq 4M})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,\delta,j}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}\rangle^{-(2-2\delta)(1+j)+p-2+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.143)

Again, this estimate and the estimate (
4.4
) with
\tilde{\ell}=\mathfrak{s}+1
yields the estimate (
4.122a
).

Last, we consider the
\mathfrak{s}
mode. We utilize the global
r^{p}
estimate (
4.116
) with

. Using the estimates (

) for the last fourth, third and second terms and (

) for the last term, the last four terms on the RHS are bounded by

\displaystyle\tau_{1}^{-2+C\delta+p-4}\Big(

\displaystyle F^{(0)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),4-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})+F^{(1)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}+1})

\displaystyle+F^{(1)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+2})+F^{(0)}(k+k^{\prime}(j),2-\delta,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\Big),

therefore, we obtain for any
p\in[4,5-\delta]
that

\displaystyle\hskip-30.1388ptF^{(0)}(k,p,\tau_{2},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})+F^{(0)}(k,2-\delta,\tau_{2},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}{\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}})+\lVert\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{s}}\rVert^{2}_{W_{p-5}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{\tau_{2},\infty}^{\geq 4M})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,\delta,j}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}\rangle^{-5-(2-2\delta)j+C\delta+p}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.144)

where we have utilized the estimate (
4.4
) to include the term
F^{(0)}(k,2-\delta,\tau_{2},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}{\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}})
on the LHS.
Together with the estimate (
4.4
), we achieve the estimate (
4.122d
) for any
p\in[2+\delta,5-\delta]
and the estimate (
4.122c
) for any
p\in[\delta,2-\delta]
.

In the end, we consider the modes of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component. Note from Proposition
3.18
that the scalar
(\hat{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(2\mathfrak{s}+i)})_{\tilde{\ell}}
satisfies the same wave equation as the one of the scalar
(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{\tilde{\ell}}
and from Proposition
3.19
that the scalar

\tilde{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s},\ell}
satisfies the same equation as the one of scalar
\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},\ell}
. As a consequence, we have analogous estimates for the modes of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component as the estimates (
4.122
) for the modes of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component. That is, we have for any
\tau_{1}>\tau_{1}^{\prime}\geq\tau_{0}
and any
p\in[\delta,2-\delta]
that

\displaystyle F^{(2\mathfrak{s})}(k,p,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}+1})\lesssim_{k,\delta,j}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-6-2j+p+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]

\displaystyle F^{(2\mathfrak{s})}(k,p,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+2})\lesssim_{k,\delta,j}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-6-2j+p+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]

\displaystyle F^{(2\mathfrak{s})}(k,p,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\lesssim_{k,\delta,j}{}

\displaystyle\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-5-2j+p+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}],

and the estimate (
4.123
) for any
p\in[2+\delta,5-\delta]
holds. We take
p=2-\delta
in the above estimates to attain energy decay for
F^{(2\mathfrak{s})}(k,p,\tau_{1},\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\tilde{\ell}})
for
\tilde{\ell}\in\{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{s}+1,\geq\mathfrak{s}+2\}

(specifically,
\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-4-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
for
\tilde{\ell}\in\{\mathfrak{s}+1,\geq\mathfrak{s}+2\}
and
\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-3-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
for
\tilde{\ell}=\mathfrak{s}
), and taking
p=\delta
in the above estimates and summing up together yields
\langle\tau_{1}-\tau_{1}^{\prime}\rangle^{-5-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{1}^{\prime}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
decay for
F^{(2\mathfrak{s})}(k,\delta,\tau_{1},\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})
, thus we plug these two energy decay estimates back to (
4.4
) with
\tau_{1}=\frac{\tau_{2}+\tau_{1}^{\prime}}{2}
to conclude the rest estimates in (
4.123
).
∎

4.5.
Almost sharp decay for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components

We derive the almost sharp pointwise decay estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components in this subsection.

To begin with, we make use of the energy decay estimates in Proposition
4.24
to derive some weaker (than almost sharp) pointwise decay for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components.

Corollary 4.25
.

For the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-1}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.145a)

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-1}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.145b)

For the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-1}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.146a)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-1}(\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.146b)

Further, we have for
\rho\geq 3M
,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}{}

\displaystyle v^{-1+C_{j}\delta}\tau^{-j}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.147a)

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\tilde{\Phi}_{-\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}{}

\displaystyle v^{-1+C_{j}\delta}\tau^{-j}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.147b)

Proof.

Note from equations (
4.122
) that for
\mathfrak{s}+1
and
\geq\mathfrak{s}+2
modes, the energies and the spacetime integrals have the same decay, hence we arrive at the same decay estimates for
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
. Then, an application of the Sobolev inequality (
2.41
) with
\alpha=\delta
yields

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.148)

and applying the other Sobolev inequality (
2.42
) yields

\displaystyle\lvert r^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}\tau^{-\frac{7}{2}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.149)

The above two estimates then prove the pointwise decay estimate (
4.145b
) in regions
\{r\geq\tau\}
and
\{r\leq\tau\}
respectively. The rest estimates are proven in the same manner and we omit the proof.
∎

In the following two subsubsections, we will refine these pointwise decay estimates (
4.145
) and (
4.146
) in the exterior region
\{\rho\geq\tau\}
and the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
, respectively, such that the decay estimates for the
\mathfrak{s}
mode are close to the sharp decay (i.e. the Price’s law decay), and the decay of the
\mathfrak{s}+1
and
\geq\mathfrak{s}+2
modes are faster than the Price’s law for the entire spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components but slower than the expected Price’s law of the modes themselves.

We state the almost sharp decay estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components here.

Proposition 4.26

(Almost sharp pointwise decay estimates for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components)
.

Let
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
.
For the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-2\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1-2\mathfrak{s}}\tau^{-2-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.150a)

and for
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(r^{-2\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1-2\mathfrak{s}}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.150b)

For the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, we have for the
\mathfrak{s}
mode that

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.151a)

and for
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes that

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.151b)

Moreover, in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
, we have for
\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}
, the radial derivative of the
\mathfrak{s}
mode of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, the following decay:

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\mu r\partial_{\rho})^{k}(\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert\lesssim_{j,k,\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-3-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.152)

This proposition will be proven in the following two subsubsections in the exterior and interior regions respectively. We shall remark that in both regions, the TSI in Section
3.4
will be of crucial importance in deriving the decay estimates for one spin component from the ones of the other spin component, an observation been already made in
[
71
]
.

4.5.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.26
in the exterior region
\{\rho\geq\tau\}

Note first that in the exterior region
\{\rho\geq\tau\}
, it holds
r\gtrsim v
, hence the estimates (
4.150
) for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component are valid.

It remains to show the estimates (
4.151
) for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, and this is achieved by make using of the estimates (
4.150
) for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component together with the TSI (
3.196b
) and (
3.198b
).

Consider only the more complicated
\mathfrak{s}=2
case (because of the presence of an extra term
12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{-2}}
in (
3.198b
)), and the simpler case
\mathfrak{s}=1
can be treated in the same way.
Recall the TSI (
3.198b
). Commuting
j
times with the Killing vector
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}
and using the formula
Y=\mu^{-1}(2\mathcal{L}_{\xi}+\frac{2a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}-r^{-1}rV)
, it can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathring{\eth}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}\leq 4}O(1)(r^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\eta})^{j_{1}}(rV)^{j_{2}}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{j_{3}}(r^{-4}\psi_{+2})

\displaystyle-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\psi_{-2}}+\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\leq 4,\,\,j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 4}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j_{1}}\mathring{\eth}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}.

(4.153)

The
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}
norms of the first line of the RHS is bounded by
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-4-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{+2}]
from (
4.145
), and the ones of the second line is bounded by
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-2-1-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}[\Psi_{-2}]
from (
4.146
), hence

\displaystyle\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-5-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.154)

Since by (
2.23
) there is a trivial kernel for the operator
\mathring{\eth}^{4}
when acting on spin
-2
scalars, we can thus apply elliptic estimates to the LHS and conclude

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-5-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.155)

Now we have obtained an extra
\tau^{-1}
decay for
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}
compared to the decay estimate (
4.146
), and we can run the above argument again except that we now use (
4.155
) instead of the decay estimates (
4.146
) to estimate the second line of (
4.5.1
).
This allows us to achieve

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-6-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.156)

In particular, the TSI (
4.5.1
)
can now be written as

\displaystyle\mathring{\eth}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}-Y^{4}(\psi_{+2})={}

\displaystyle-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\psi_{-2}}+\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\leq 4,\,\,j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 4}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j_{1}}\mathring{\eth}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2},

(4.157)

the absolute value of the RHS of which is bounded by
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-7-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
.

Our next step is to first project the TSI (
4.157
) onto the
\mathfrak{s}
mode and
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes, and this leads to the following TSI in the mode level:

\displaystyle\mathring{\eth}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-2})_{\mathfrak{s}}-(Y^{4}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{+2}))_{\mathfrak{s}}={}\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{s}}\Big(-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\psi_{-2}}+\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\leq 4,\,\,j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 4}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j_{1}}\mathring{\eth}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}\Big),

(4.158a)

\displaystyle\mathring{\eth}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-2})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}-(Y^{4}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{+2}))_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}={}\mathbf{P}_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\Big(-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\psi_{-2}}+\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\leq 4,\,\,j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 4}O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j_{1}}\mathring{\eth}^{j_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{-2}\Big).

(4.158b)

The
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}
norms of the RHS of both (
4.158a
) and (
4.158b
) are bounded by
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-7-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
, and by the estimates (
4.150
), we have

\displaystyle\lvert(Y^{4}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{+2}))_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}{}

\displaystyle v^{-1}\tau^{-6-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.159a)

\displaystyle\lvert(Y^{4}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\psi_{+2}))_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}{}

\displaystyle v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{13}{2}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.159b)

Therefore, by an elliptic estimate (which is again due to the trivial kernel of
\mathring{\eth}^{4}
when acting on a spin
-2
scalar), we prove the decay estimates (
4.151
) for the spin
-2
component in the exterior region
\{\rho\geq\tau\}
.
∎

4.5.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.26
in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}

Before passing to the detailed proof, we provide an outline of the proof. The proof of Proposition
4.26
in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
is divided into four steps. The first two steps are to obtain different types of elliptic estimates for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component: the first step is to make use of subsystems of (
3.132
) for
\mathfrak{s}=1
or of (
3.143
) for
\mathfrak{s}=2
, isolate out the spin-weighted angular elliptic parts, and apply elliptic estimates to achieve faster
r^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component than the decay estimates in Corollary
4.25
; while the second step is to write the TME (
3.30
) for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component as a three dimensional elliptic (but only in a region a bit far away from horizon) equation in space and, nevertheless, achieve elliptic estimates such that we can improve the above
r^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay to
\tau^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay, thus proving the almost sharp decay (
4.151
) for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component. As a byproduct, we obtain in the third step that the radial derivative of the
\mathfrak{s}
mode of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component has extra
\tau^{-1}
decay. In the last step, we utilize these almost sharp decay for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component together with the TSI and the proven estimates for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component in Corollary
4.25
to deduce the almost sharp decay for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component.

Step 1
. Our first step is to derive elliptic estimates for subsystems of (
3.132
) for
\mathfrak{s}=1
and of (
3.143
) for
\mathfrak{s}=2
to achieve further
r^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay for the modes of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component. The main estimates we shall prove in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
are as follows:
for the
\mathfrak{s}
mode,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}r^{-\mathfrak{s}}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.160a)

and for
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}r^{-\mathfrak{s}}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.160b)

The above estimates (
4.160
) in the case
\mathfrak{s}=0
are already contained in the estimates (
4.146
). We shall prove only
\mathfrak{s}=1
and
\mathfrak{s}=2
cases.

Let us first consider the case
\mathfrak{s}=1
. By the expression (
3.3
) of
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
, we can recast the first subequation of (
3.132
) in the region
\{3M\leq\rho\leq\tau\}
as

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-2)\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}={}

\displaystyle((r^{2}+a^{2})YV-2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}-2ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}+\frac{4ar}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}-\frac{a^{2}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}}\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{-1}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})rV\Psi_{-1}^{(1)}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-1}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-1}^{(1)}+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{-1}^{(0)}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-1}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-1}^{(0)}+O(r^{-2})\Psi_{-1}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\mu(a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}+2ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Psi_{-1}^{(0)},

(4.161)

where in the second step we have used the definition
\Phi_{-1}^{(1)}=\mu^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})V\Phi_{-1}^{(0)}
and all the
O(\cdot)
coefficients are
\theta
-independent. By projecting this equation onto
\mathfrak{s}
mode and
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes and applying elliptic estimates on sphere, and noticing that the terms on the RHS either are with
r^{-1}
decay coefficient or contain
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}
derivative that yields an extra
\tau^{-1}
decay (thus extra
r^{-1}
decay since
r\leq\tau
) by Corollary
4.25
, the estimates (
4.160
) follow.

Then consider
\mathfrak{s}=2
. Again, in the region
\{3M\leq\rho\leq\tau\}
, we use the expression (
3.3
) of
\widehat{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\displaystyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\textstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle#$\hfil\cr\square\cr{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}}\crcr}}}}_{s}
and the definition
\Phi_{-1}^{(i+1)}=\mu^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})V\Phi_{-1}^{(i)}
to rewrite the first two subequations of (
3.143
) into

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-4)\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle((r^{2}+a^{2})YV-2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}-4ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+O(r^{-1})\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})rV\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}

\displaystyle-\mu^{2}(a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}+4ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Psi_{-2},

(4.162a)

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-6)\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}+6M\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+6a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle((r^{2}+a^{2})YV-2a\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}-4ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Phi_{-2}^{(2)}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}+O(r^{-1})\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Psi_{-2}^{(2)}+O(r^{-1})rV\Psi_{-2}^{(2)}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}^{(2)}+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}^{(2)}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}^{(1)}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Psi_{-2}+O(r^{-1})\Psi_{-2}

\displaystyle-(a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{2}+4ia\cos\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}.

(4.162b)

The LHS of the system (
4.162
) can be written as
\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-4&0\\
6M+6a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}&\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-6\end{smallmatrix}\big)\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\Phi_{-2}^{(0)}\\
\Phi_{-2}^{(1)}\end{smallmatrix}\big)
, and this
2\times 2
matrix is lower triangular and has nonzero eigenvalues. Therefore, by the same argument of projecting this equation onto
\mathfrak{s}
mode and
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes, applying elliptic estimates on sphere in Section
2.4
and noticing that the terms on the RHS either are with
r^{-1}
decay coefficient or contain
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}
derivative that yields an extra
\tau^{-1}
decay (thus extra
r^{-1}
decay since
r\leq\tau
) by Corollary
4.25
, we achieve extra
r^{-1}
decay compared to the ones in (
4.151
). That is, the following holds for
\mathfrak{s}=2
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0,1}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((r^{-1}\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}r^{-1}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.163a)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0,1}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((r^{-1}\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}r^{-1}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.163b)

Given these estimates, we now apply the same argument to the single equation (
4.162
), and for the same reason, we can derive extra
r^{-1}
decay for
(r^{-1}\Psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)})_{\mathfrak{s}}
compared to the ones in (
4.163
), hence completing the proof of the estimates (
4.160
) in the case
j=0
. Commuting the equations used in this step with
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}
then proves the estimates (
4.160
) for general
j\in\mathbb{N}
.

Step 2
. This second step is to prove the almost sharp decay estimates (
4.151
) for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component in the interior region by a different type of elliptic estimate. This other type of elliptic estimates in
3
-dimensional space allows us to trade the achieved extra
r^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay in the previous step for extra
\tau^{-\mathfrak{s}}
decay.

Our main estimates to show in this step are as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\rho\leq\tau}r^{-1+2\delta}(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2})\mathrm{d}\rho\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}{}\tau^{-6-4\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}},

(4.164a)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{-1+2\delta}\big(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}+\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{j,\delta,k}{}\tau^{-7-4\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.164b)

The pointwise decay estimates (
4.151
) then follow easily from the Sobolev inequality (
2.40
) applied to these energy decay estimates. As a result, the remaining discussions in this step are devoted to proving the estimates (
4.164
).

Recall equation (
3.30
). We take
s=-\mathfrak{s}
in equation (
3.30
), commute with
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}

and project onto
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes,
arriving at

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}+\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\mathbf{P}_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.165)

For ease of notation, we denote
\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}=(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}
and
H_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}=\mathbf{P}_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
. The above equation then becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})+2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}+\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}.

(4.166)

We multiply
2f\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\overline{\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}}
on both sides and take the real part, then by Leibniz’s rule, we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt\partial_{\rho}(f\lvert\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}-f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2})-\partial_{r}f\lvert\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\partial_{r}(f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1})\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt+\Re(\mathring{\eth}(2f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\overline{\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}}))+\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(2af\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\lvert\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Re(2f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\cdot\partial_{\rho}\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}}).

(4.167)

We then take
f=\mu^{-2\mathfrak{s}-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\beta}
with
0<\beta<2\mathfrak{s}+1
in the above formula and integrate the formula in
\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}
. Note that the boundary term at
\rho=r_{+}
vanishes since

\displaystyle\hskip-12.91663pt\big(f\lvert\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}-f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\big)|_{\rho=r_{+}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(\mu(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2\mathfrak{s}-\beta+2}\lvert\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}-\mu(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2\mathfrak{s}-\beta+1}\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2})|_{\rho=r_{+}}=0,

and the integral of the second line vanishes.
Further,

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}f={}(2\mathfrak{s}+1)\partial_{r}\mu\mu^{-2\mathfrak{s}-2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\beta}+2\beta\mu^{-2\mathfrak{s}-1}r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\beta-1}\gtrsim_{\beta}\mu^{-2\mathfrak{s}-2}r^{-2\beta-1},

(4.168a)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(f\Delta^{2\mathfrak{s}+1})={}2(2\mathfrak{s}-\beta+1)r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2\mathfrak{s}-\beta}\gtrsim_{\beta}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+1},

(4.168b)

\displaystyle\int_{S^{2}}\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mu\geq{}2(\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{S^{2}}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mu,

(4.168c)

where the last inequality follows from (
2.31
).
Hence, an application of Cauchy–Schwarz to the integral of the RHS of (
4.5.2
) then yields for any
0<\beta<2\mathfrak{s}+1
,

\displaystyle\hskip-21.52771pt\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+1}\big(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}{}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+1}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu+\Big(\int_{S^{2}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+2}\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mu\Big)\Big|_{\rho=\tau}.

(4.169)

We can also treat the
\mathfrak{s}
mode in an exactly same way. Taking
s=-\mathfrak{s}
in equation (
3.30
), commuting with
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}

and projecting onto an
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode,
we arrive at

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.170)

For ease of notation, we denote
\varphi_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
,
H_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
,
\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}=(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}
and
H_{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{s}}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
, and recast the above equation as

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{m,\mathfrak{s}})+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{m,\mathfrak{s}}.

(4.171)

The only difference between this equation and equation (
4.166
) lies in the angular derivative term.
With the same discussions, one achieves for any
0<\beta<2\mathfrak{s}+1
,

\displaystyle\hskip-21.52771pt\Big(\int_{S^{2}}\mu r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+2}\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mu\Big)\Big|_{\rho=\tau}+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+1}(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2})\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}{}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+1}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}\rho+\Big(\int_{S^{2}}r^{4\mathfrak{s}-2\beta+2}\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}^{2}\mu\Big)\Big|_{\rho=\tau}.

(4.172)

Here, we have summed over
m
with
\lvert m\rvert\leq\mathfrak{s}
and used the Hardy’s inequality (
2.39b
).

By the expression (
3.6
) of
H[\psi_{s}]
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}\lesssim_{k}{}

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}(r\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}})\rvert_{k+1,\mathbb{D}}^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k+1,\mathbb{D}}^{2},

(4.173a)

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}H_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}^{2}\lesssim_{k}{}

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}(r\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1})\rvert_{k+1,\mathbb{D}}^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert_{k+1,\mathbb{D}}^{2}.

(4.173b)

We first take
\beta=\mathfrak{s}+1-\delta
in both (
4.5.2
) and (
4.5.2
). In view of the estimate (
4.173a
) and the pointwise estimates (
4.160
), the RHS of (
4.5.2
) is bounded by
C_{j,\delta}\tau^{-6-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
, thus arriving at

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{2\mathfrak{s}-1+2\delta}(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2})\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu\lesssim_{j,\delta}{}

\displaystyle\tau^{-6-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.174a)

We can now utilize this estimate, the estimate (
4.173b
) and the pointwise estimates (
4.160
) to find the the RHS of (
4.5.2
) is bounded by
C_{j,\delta}\tau^{-7-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
, which yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{2\mathfrak{s}-1+2\delta}\big(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu\lesssim_{j,\delta}{}

\displaystyle\tau^{-7-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.174b)

Next, we take
\beta=\mathfrak{s}+2-\delta
in both (
4.5.2
) and (
4.5.2
). The same argument applies except that we shall use (
4.174
) instead of (
4.160
) to control the RHS of (
4.5.2
); we will achieve

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{2\mathfrak{s}-3+2\delta}(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\mathfrak{s}}\rvert^{2})\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu\lesssim_{j,\delta}{}

\displaystyle\tau^{-8-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.175a)

Moreover, using this estimate together with the estimate (
4.173b
) and the pointwise estimates (
4.174
) to control the RHS of (
4.5.2
), one finds

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}}r^{2\mathfrak{s}-3+2\delta}\big(\lvert r\partial_{\rho}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}\varphi_{\geq\mathfrak{s}+1}\rvert^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}^{3}\mu\lesssim_{j,\delta}{}

\displaystyle\tau^{-9-2\mathfrak{s}-2j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.175b)

Note that the improvement of (
4.175
) compared to (
4.174
) lies in the fact that we have traded the
r
weights inside the integral on the LHS for the same amount of
\tau
decay. This argument can be inductively applied until we reach the final choice
\beta=2\mathfrak{s}+1-\delta
, and we eventually conclude the estimate (
4.164a
).
Further, using this estimate together with the estimate (
4.173b
) and the pointwise estimates (
4.174
) to control the RHS of (
4.5.2
), the estimate (
4.164
) with
k=0
follows.

We then proceed to general
k\in\mathbb{N}
case. Since
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
commute with equation (
3.30
), and since
\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}
commutes with the LHS of equation (
3.30
) and the obtained RHS enjoys the same kind of estimates as the ones in (
4.173
) (with the only difference that the RHS of (
4.173
) requires higher order regularity norms), we achieve the estimates with
\mathbb{D}
replaced by
\{\mathcal{L}_{\xi},\mathcal{L}_{\eta},\mathring{\eth},\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\}
.

Based on the above discussions, It remains to prove the estimates (
4.164
) with
\mathbb{D}
replaced by
\{\rho\partial_{\rho}\}
. We prove it by induction in
k
, that is,
assuming it holds for
k=n-1
,
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we prove for
k=n
. We multiply both sides of equation (
3.30
) by
\mu^{-\mathfrak{s}}
to get a rewritten form of equation (
3.30
):

\displaystyle\mu^{-\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}].

(4.176)

We then commute this equation with
\rho\partial_{\rho}
, and since

\displaystyle r\partial_{\rho}\Big(\mu^{-\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{1+\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\Big)={}

\displaystyle\mu^{-(\mathfrak{s}+1)}\partial_{\rho}\Big(\mu\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}(r\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\Big)

\displaystyle+\big(O_{\infty}(1)\mu r^{2\mathfrak{s}+2}\partial_{\rho}^{2}+O_{\infty}(1)r^{2\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}+O_{\infty}(1)r^{2\mathfrak{s}}\big)\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}},

(4.177)

where
O_{\infty}(1)
are
O(1)
functions and smooth everywhere in
\rho\in[r_{+},\infty)
,
we obtain
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt\mu^{-(\mathfrak{s}+n)}\partial_{\rho}\big((r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\mu^{1+(\mathfrak{s}+n)}\partial_{\rho}((r\partial_{\rho})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\big)+2a(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}((r\partial_{\rho})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}((r\partial_{\rho})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(r\partial_{\rho})^{n}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]+r^{2\mathfrak{s}}\Big(\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}\leq 1}\sum_{i_{3}\leq 1}O_{\infty}(1)(r\partial_{\rho})^{i_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{i_{2}}(\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{i_{3}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+O_{\infty}(1)\mu(r\partial_{\rho})^{n+1}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}\Big).

(4.178)

We can achieve elliptic estimates for this equation of
(r\partial_{\rho})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}
in a similar way of treating equation (
4.176
) (or equivalently, equation (
3.30
)). More specifically, by projecting the above equation onto an
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode (resp.
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes), we multiply both sides of the obtained equation by
2\mu^{\mathfrak{s}+n}f(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\mu^{1+\mathfrak{s}+n}\overline{\partial_{\rho}((r\partial_{\rho})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})}
, with
f=\mu^{-2(\mathfrak{s}+n)-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\beta}
, and integrate over
r_{+}\leq\rho\leq\tau
(resp.
\Sigma_{\tau}^{\leq\tau}
).
The integral arising from the last term of (
4.5.2
) can be estimated by the assumption in the induction together with the proven estimate (
4.164
) but with
\mathbb{D}
replaced by
\{\mathcal{L}_{\xi},\mathcal{L}_{\eta},\mathring{\eth},\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\}
, thus the same argument as the one in treating
k=0
case applies and yields the estimate (
4.164
) for
k=n
.

Step 3
. This third step is to prove the estimate (
4.152
) which encodes further decay for the radial derivative of the
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, i.e. for
\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
, in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
.

We shall need the following lemma that is immediate from Proposition
3.6
.

Lemma 4.27
.

Let

\displaystyle w=w(a,M,r,m)=e^{\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\frac{2iam}{\Delta(r^{\prime})}dr^{\prime}}.

(4.179)

The
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode
(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(w\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})={}

\displaystyle w\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\big(H[(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}]-\mathbf{C}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\big)

(4.180)

with
the term
H[(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}]
on the RHS satisfying

\displaystyle H[(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}]={}

\displaystyle O(1)r\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+O(1)(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}.

(4.181)

Proof.

By Proposition
3.6
with
s=-\mathfrak{s}
, and in view of the facts that
H_{\text{hyp}}=O(r^{-2})
and
(r-M)\mu^{-1}-r=O(r^{-1})
, we have for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component that

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+2a\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\partial_{\rho}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}],

(4.182)

with
the term
H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
on the RHS satisfying

\displaystyle H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]={}

\displaystyle O(1)r\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+O(1)\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}-2ias\cos\theta\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}.

We project this equation onto the
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode and, noticing from (
2.29
) that
\mathring{\eth}\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}((\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}Y_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}(\cos\theta)e^{im{\tilde{\phi}}})=0
, we conclude

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}={}

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\big(H[(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}]-\mathbf{C}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\big).

(4.183)

A simple rescaling then yields the desired equation.
∎

The above equation (
4.180
) can be integrated from horizon to yield a refined decay estimate for
\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
.

Proof of the estimate (
4.152
)
:
For any point
(\tau,\rho^{\prime})
satisfying
\rho^{\prime}\leq\tau
, we integrate equation (
4.180
) from horizon and obtain

\displaystyle\big(w\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\big)(\tau,\rho^{\prime})=\int_{r_{+}}^{\rho^{\prime}}w\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\big(H[(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}]-\mathbf{C}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]\big)\mathrm{d}\rho.

(4.184)

By Definition
3.17
for
\mathbf{C}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]
and the decay estimates (
4.151
), the absolute value of the RHS is bounded by
C_{\delta}\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}v^{-1}\tau^{-3-2\mathfrak{s}+C\delta})(\tau,\rho^{\prime})
, which thus yields (
4.152
) for
k=j=0
.

We next apply
\partial_{\rho}(\mu r\cdot)
on both sides of equation (
4.180
) and integrate this new obtained equation from horizon. The above proof still works and implies that

\displaystyle\lvert\partial_{\rho}(\mu r(\partial_{\rho}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{\mathfrak{s}}))\rvert\lesssim_{\delta}v^{-1}\tau^{-3-2\mathfrak{s}+C\delta}\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}.

(4.185)

This together with the estimate in the previous step completes the proof of (
4.152
) in the case
(k,j)=(1,0)
. The same argument applies to the general
(k\in\mathbb{N},j=0)
case. In the end, it is manifest that
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}
commutes with equation (
4.180
) and from the decay estimates (
4.151
),
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}
acting on the RHS of (
4.180
) has extra
\tau^{-j}
decay, hence the above argument applies and completes the proof in the general
(k,j)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
cases.

Step 4
. Our last step is to show the decay estimates (
4.150
) for the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component via the TSI together with the proven almost sharp decay estimates (
4.151
) for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component.

The proof is in fact in the same spirit of the one in Section
4.5.1
where the almost sharp decay estimates for the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component in the exterior region are proven via the almost sharp decay of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component and an application of (the other) TSI. Again, we consider only the more complicated
\mathfrak{s}=2
case, and the simpler case
\mathfrak{s}=1
can be similarly treated.

Recall the TSI (
3.198a
). Commuting with
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}
and multiplying by
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}
, it can be written as

\displaystyle(\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{4}(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\psi_{+2}))-12M\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\psi_{+2})}={}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}\hat{V}^{4}(\Delta^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-2})).

(4.186)

By the decay estimates (
4.151
) for the modes of the spin
-\mathfrak{s}
component, we find that
if projecting this equation onto the
\mathfrak{s}
mode, the
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}
norm of the RHS is bounded by
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-2-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
; instead, if projecting this equation onto the
\geq\mathfrak{s}+1
modes, the
\lvert\cdot\rvert_{k,\mathbb{D}}
norm of the RHS has decay
C_{j,\delta,k}v^{-1}\tau^{-\frac{5}{2}-2\mathfrak{s}-j+C_{j}\delta}\textbf{I}^{k+k^{\prime}(j),\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}
. The remaining discussions are exactly the same as the ones in Section
4.5.1
and will be dropped; these will prove the estimates (
4.150
) but with the factor
v^{-1-2\mathfrak{s}}
on the RHS replaced by
v^{-1}\tau^{-2\mathfrak{s}}
. However, in the interior region
\{\rho\leq\tau\}
, we have
\tau\gtrsim v
, hence the estimates (
4.150
) hold.
∎

5.
Global sharp decay of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components

In this section, we will prove the sharp decay for the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components using the almost sharp decay estimates proven in the previous section. In Section
5.1
, we deduce for the
(m,\mathfrak{s})
-mode of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component a global conservation law, which allows us to calculate the integral of its radiation field along the future null infinity. This conservation law is then utilized in Section
5.2
to derive the precise asymptotic profile of this mode in separate regions
\{r\geq v^{\alpha}\}
and
\{r\leq v^{\alpha}\}
for some
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.

Throughout this section, the BEAM estimates assumption
4.2
for an inhomogeneous TEM is always assumed.
Therefore, in view of Remark
4.4
, all the estimates in Section
4
are valid for
\mathfrak{s}=0
in any subextreme Kerr and
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
in slowly rotating Kerr with
\lvert a\rvert/M
sufficiently small, and are valid for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
in any subextreme Kerr under Assumption
4.2
.

5.1.
Global conservation law

The main result of this subsection is to compute the integral of the radiation field of any
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component on future null infinity with respect to the initial data. This is achieve by a global conservation law for the TME of this mode.

Recall equation (
3.34
) of
\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}
in Corollary
3.7
. By projecting this equation onto an
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\varphi+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi)=\partial_{\tau}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])

(5.1)

where we have used equation (
2.22
) and denoted
\varphi=(\varphi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
. For further analysis, we expand
\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])\\
=&-2\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}}-1)\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})\\
&-(r^{2}+a^{2})\mu H_{\text{hyp}}(H_{\text{hyp}}-2\mu^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+2iam\big[1+(H_{\text{hyp}}-2\mu^{-1})\big](\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\\
&-\big[(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})+2s((r-M)(2\mu^{-1}-H_{\text{hyp}})-2r)\big](\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\\
&-\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})+\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\\
=&2\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}-2\mu H_{\text{hyp}}\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\rho}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}})\\
&-\mu(r^{2}+a^{2})(H_{\text{hyp}})^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+[2iamH_{\text{hyp}}-(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{r}(\mu H_{\text{hyp}})](\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}},\end{split}

(5.2)

with
\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}=\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\big(\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)}\big)\big)
by the definition in Proposition (
3.19
).
Further, from formula (
5.2
), one finds that
\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])
is smooth up to and including horizon and it holds as
\rho\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])=\frac{2\mu^{\mathfrak{s}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}+O(r^{-2})r\partial_{\rho}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+O(r^{-2})(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}.

(5.3)

Before stating the conservation law, we introduce some notations and calculate the both sides of the TSI (
3.196a
) and (
3.198a
) in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

•

For
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
, we have for the RHS of the TSI (
3.196a
) and (
3.198a
) that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\hat{V}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2\mathfrak{s}}\tilde{u}_{-\mathfrak{s},j}(r)V^{j}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}

\displaystyle\doteq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{j}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},j})(r^{2}+a^{2})^{j}(r-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-j}u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}(r)V^{j}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}}u_{-\mathfrak{s},0}(r)\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}},

(5.4)

where
\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},j}=0
except that
\delta_{-2,2}=10
and
\delta_{-2,1}=20
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}(r)=1+u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}^{(1)}(r)\mu+u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}^{(2)}(r)\mu^{2},\ \quad\text{for}\ \ j=1,\cdots,2\mathfrak{s},\\
&u_{-\mathfrak{s},0}(r)=(2\mathfrak{s})!\mu^{\mathfrak{s}},\end{split}

(5.5)

with
u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}^{(1)}(r)=O(1)
and
u_{-\mathfrak{s},j}^{(2)}(r)=O(1)
being smooth functions up to and including horizon.

•

On
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we can expand
\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\hat{V}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
as follow:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\hat{V}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\big|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=\sum_{n=1}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{n}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},n})(r_{+}-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-n}(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta})^{n}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}+\sum_{j+k\leq 2\mathfrak{s}-1}\mathfrak{b}_{-\mathfrak{s},j,k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j+1}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{k}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}

(5.6)

where
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{s},j,k}=(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{j+1+k}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},j+1+k})C_{j+1+k}^{j+1}2^{2j+2+k}a^{k}(Mr_{+})^{j+1}(r_{+}-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-1-j-k}
.

•

For
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
and
|m|\leq\mathfrak{s}
,
let
\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
be the unique differential operator such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})=\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}\big((\mathring{\eth}^{\prime}-ia\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi})^{2\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-12M(\mathfrak{s}-1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\overline{\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}}\big)-\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}\big((\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{2\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\big).

(5.7)

Then the
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode projection form of the TSI (
3.196a
) and (
3.198a
) for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
becomes

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}\big((\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{2\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\big)=\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\big(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\hat{V}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})\big).

(5.8)

Proof.

In fact, one can expand out
\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\hat{V}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})
and obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{V}^{2}(\Delta\psi_{-1})=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}V^{2}\psi_{-1}+(2(r-M)(r^{2}+a^{2})+2r\Delta)V\psi_{-1}+2\Delta\psi_{-1}

(5.9a)

and

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}\hat{V}^{4}(\Delta^{2}\psi_{-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}V^{4}\psi_{-2}+\Big[4(r-M)(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}+12r(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\Delta\Big]V^{3}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle+\Big[-4(r-M)^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}+(40r(r-M)+16)(r^{2}+a^{2})\Delta+(20r^{2}+8a^{2})\Delta^{2}\Big]V^{2}\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle+\Big[-16(r-M)^{3}(r^{2}+a^{2})+(40(r^{2}+a^{2})+16r(r-M))(r-M)\Delta+(56r-20M)\Delta^{2}\Big]V\psi_{-2}

\displaystyle+24\Delta^{2}\psi_{-2}.

(5.9b)

Formula (
•
‣
5.1
) then follows. By restricting these equations on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and using
V\big|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=2\mathcal{L}_{\xi}+\frac{a}{Mr_{+}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
, one immediately achieves equation (
5.6
).
∎

In the theorem below, a global conservation law is derived, and using this conservation law, the integral of the radiation field of an
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component along future null infinity is calculated in terms of the initial data of the spin
\pm\mathfrak{s}
components on
\Sigma_{\tau_{0}}
.

Theorem 5.2

(Global conservation law)
.

Assume
\textbf{I}^{k,\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}<+\infty
for a sufficiently small
\delta>0
and some suitably large integer
k
. Then, we have for
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
and
|m|\leq\mathfrak{s}
the following conservation law

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 17.22217pt&\hskip-17.22217pt(2\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
=&-[2iam-2\mathfrak{s}(r_{+}-M)]\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])(\rho,\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}\rho\end{split}

(5.10)

and the following expression of the value of the integral of
(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
along future null infinity
11
11

11

Note that for
\mathfrak{s}=0
(hence
(m=0)
), the above formula (
5.10
) already provides the value of integral of
(\Phi_{0})_{0,0}
along future null infinity in terms of the initial hypersurface integral
\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\mathbf{P}_{0,0}(H[\psi_{0}])(\rho,\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}\rho
.

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt(2\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])(\rho,\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2iam-2\mathfrak{s}(r_{+}-M)}{(2\mathfrak{s})!}\Big\{\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})(\tau_{0},r_{+})-\sum_{j+k\leq 2\mathfrak{s}-1}(im)^{k}\mathfrak{b}_{-\mathfrak{s},j,k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau_{0},r_{+})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathfrak{c}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}(r)w^{-1}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}w(r^{\prime})\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(r^{\prime})\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])(r^{\prime},\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r\Big\},

(5.11)

where
\mathfrak{c}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}=\sum_{n=1}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{n}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},n})(r_{+}-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-n}(2iam)^{n}
for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
and
\mathfrak{c}_{0,0}=1
,
\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})
and
\mathfrak{b}_{-\mathfrak{s},j,k}
are defined as in Lemma
5.1
for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
and
\mathfrak{a}_{0,0}(\psi_{0})=0
for
\mathfrak{s}=0
, and
w(r)=e^{\int_{r_{+}}^{r}\frac{2iam}{\Delta(r^{\prime})}dr^{\prime}}
as defined in (
4.179
).

Proof.

Step 1. Conservation law.

By assumption and the estimates (
4.145a
) and (
4.147a
), there exits a small
\delta
such that
|\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}|\lesssim v^{-1+\delta}(\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\rho\partial_{\rho})^{i}(\Psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\lesssim\tau^{-2-j+\delta}(\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}})^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
i,j\leq 1
, which suggest

\displaystyle|\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])|\lesssim_{\delta}(\rho^{-1-\varepsilon}v^{-1+\delta+\varepsilon}+\rho^{-2}\tau^{-2+\delta})(\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}})^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.12)

by formula (
5.2
).

We integrate equation (
5.1
) in
\mathcal{D}(\tau_{0},\tau^{\prime},r^{\prime})=\{(\tau,\rho)|\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau^{\prime},r_{+}\leq\rho\leq r^{\prime}\}
and obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt\int_{r_{+}}^{r^{\prime}}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])(\rho,\tau^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\rho-\int_{r_{+}}^{r^{\prime}}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])(\rho,\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}\rho+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\varphi+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi)|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{\rho}\varphi+2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}\varphi)(\tau,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\bigg(\frac{\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}+\frac{3}{2}}}(r^{2}+a^{2})\partial_{\rho}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+\bigg(\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}+1}\partial_{r}((r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\mathfrak{s}-\frac{1}{2}})+\frac{2iam\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{\mathfrak{s}+\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\bigg)(\tau,r^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.13)

The first term on the LHS is bounded by
C_{\delta}(\tau^{\prime})^{-1+\delta+\varepsilon}(\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}})^{\frac{1}{2}}
in view of the above bound (
5.12
) for
\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])
.
Further, taking
r^{\prime}\to\infty
, and by the boundedness of both
|(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(1)})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|
and
|(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|
, the RHS equals
(2\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau
, and the last term in the first line equals
-\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}[2iam-2\mathfrak{s}(r_{+}-M)](\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau
. In total, we achieve

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt(2\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}}])(\rho,\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}\rho-\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}[2iam-2\mathfrak{s}(r_{+}-M)](\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau+O((\tau^{\prime})^{-1+\delta+\varepsilon}).

(5.14)

The almost sharp decay estimate (
4.150a
) ensures that we can directly take the limit
\tau^{\prime}\to\infty
, and this yields the global conservation law (
5.10
) in the black hole exterior region.

Step 2. Calculating the integral along future null infinity in terms of the initial data.

Now, we are going to compute the first term in the last line of (
5.10
). That is to say, we shall calculate the integral of
(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
along the event horizon.
For
\mathfrak{s}=0
, we can directly calculate the integral expression of
(\psi_{0})_{0,0}
, while for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
, we should instead first calculate the integral expression of
(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
and then utilize the TSI to determine the value of the horizon integral of
(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
.

For
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
, we first use the TSI to calculate the horizon integral of
(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
from the horizon integral of
(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
. Recall the mode projection form (
5.8
) of the TSI. By restricting (
5.8
) on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and using (
5.6
), and by (
2.23
) that indicates
\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}^{-\mathfrak{s}}((\mathring{\eth}^{\prime})^{2\mathfrak{s}}\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})=(2\mathfrak{s})!(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 17.22217pt&\hskip-17.22217pt(2\mathfrak{s})!(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\\
={}&\sum_{n=1}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{n}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},n})(r_{+}-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-n}(2iam)^{n}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Big(\sum_{j+k\leq 2\mathfrak{s}-1}(im)^{k}\mathfrak{b}_{-\mathfrak{s},j,k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\Big).\end{split}

(5.15)

Integrating this equation along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we get

\displaystyle(2\mathfrak{s})!\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau={}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\psi_{+\mathfrak{s}})(\tau_{0},r_{+})-\sum_{j+k\leq 2\mathfrak{s}-1}(im)^{k}\mathfrak{b}_{-\mathfrak{s},j,k}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{j}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau_{0},r_{+})

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{2\mathfrak{s}}(C_{2\mathfrak{s}}^{n}-\delta_{-\mathfrak{s},n})(r_{+}-M)^{2\mathfrak{s}-n}(2iam)^{n}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.16)

It remains to calculate the last term on the RHS of (
5.1
), i.e. the horizon integral of
(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
. By (
4.180
), for
\rho>r_{+}
and
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
, we have

\displaystyle(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\rho,\tau)=-\int_{\rho}^{+\infty}\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}(r)w^{-1}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}w(r^{\prime})\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(r^{\prime})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])(r^{\prime},\tau)\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r,

(5.17)

the integral on the RHS of which is well-defined for any fixed
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
since
H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}]=O(\rho^{-1})
as
\rho
sufficiently large. Further, it is easy to show that the integral in (
5.17
) is continuous up to and including horizon,
hence, formula (
5.17
) holds on
\rho=r_{+}
as well. By integrating (
5.17
) in
\tau
on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we conclude for
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}(\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\tau=\int_{r_{+}}^{+\infty}\Delta^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}(r)w^{-1}(r)\int_{r_{+}}^{r}w(r^{\prime})\Delta^{\mathfrak{s}}(r^{\prime})\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(H[\psi_{-\mathfrak{s}}])(r^{\prime},\tau_{0})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\mathrm{d}r,

(5.18)

since the value as
\tau\to\infty
vanishes by the estimate (
5.12
).

In the end, for
\mathfrak{s}=1,2
, we substitute (
5.1
) and (
5.18
) into (
5.10
) to achieve (
5.2
), while for
\mathfrak{s}=0
, it suffices to substitute only (
5.18
) with
\mathfrak{s}=0
into (
5.10
).
∎

Additionally, we are also able to compute the integrals of
(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}
,
\ell>\mathfrak{s}
and
0\leq j<\ell-\mathfrak{s}
, on future null infinity.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
. Assume
\textbf{I}^{k,\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}<+\infty
for a sufficiently small
\delta>0
and some suitably large integer
k
. Then,
for
\ell>\mathfrak{s}
and
0\leq j<\ell-\mathfrak{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau={}&\frac{2}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s}-j)(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+j+1)}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}(\tau_{0},\rho)\\
&-\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}\sum_{n=0}^{j-j^{\prime}}\frac{2(im)^{n}x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s}-j^{\prime})(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+j^{\prime}+1)}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j^{\prime})})_{m,\ell}(\tau_{0},\rho),\end{split}

(5.19)

where

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}=\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{V}}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}\big)\Big).

(5.20)

Proof.

Similar to the proof in Proposition
3.19
, we rewrite (
3.18
) as

\displaystyle\mu Y(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}+[(\ell+\mathfrak{s})(\ell-\mathfrak{s}+1)-(2\mathfrak{s}+i)(i+1)](\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}+O(r^{-1})=0.

(5.21)

Since
\mu Y((\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell})=2\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}+O(r^{-1})rV(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}+O(r^{-2})\mathcal{L}_{\eta}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}
,
by integrating the above equation from
\tau_{0}
to
\tau^{\prime}
and taking
\rho\to\infty
, we achieve

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}(\ell-\mathfrak{s}-i)(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+i+1)\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau={}

\displaystyle-2\Big(\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\Big)\Big|_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau^{\prime}}.

(5.22)

We then take
\tau^{\prime}\to+\infty
, and since
(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)
decays in
\tau
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau=\frac{2}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s}-i)(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+i+1)}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)})_{m,\ell}(\tau_{0},\rho).

(5.23)

In the end, in view of
the definition of
\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)}
in Proposition
3.16
which reads

\displaystyle(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}={}

\displaystyle(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}+\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{j-1}\sum_{n=0}^{j-j^{\prime}}(im)^{n}x_{\mathfrak{s},j,j^{\prime},n}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j^{\prime})})_{m,\ell},

(5.24)

formula (
5.19
) then follows.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

In particular, if the initial data on
\Sigma_{\tau_{0}}
are compactly supported or decay sufficiently faster as
\rho\to+\infty
, then equality (
5.19
) actually implies
\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(j)})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau=0
for any
\ell>\mathfrak{s}
and
0\leq j<\ell-\mathfrak{s}
.

5.2.
Proof of the sharp decay

To show the sharp decay (i.e. the Price’s law), we will frequently use the coordinates
(u,v,\theta,\tilde{\phi})
, and the partial derivatives
\partial_{u}
and
\partial_{v}
shall be understood in this coordinate system. In this
(u,v,\theta,{\tilde{\phi}})
coordinate system, we can express
\partial_{u}
and
\partial_{v}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{u}=\frac{1}{2}\mu Y,\quad\partial_{v}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{V}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(V-\frac{2a}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Big).

(5.25)

The following lemma lists some useful relations and estimates among
u
,
v
,
r
, and
\tau
that are utilized in different regions in our proof for sharp decay estimates. The proof is simple and omitted.

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, let
\gamma_{\alpha}=\{r=v^{\alpha}\}
. For any
u
and
v
, let
u_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(v)
and
v_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(u)
be such that
(u_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(v),v),(u,v_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(u))\in\gamma_{\alpha}
.
In the region
r\geq v^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle r\gtrsim{}v^{\alpha}+u^{\alpha},

(5.26a)

\displaystyle\lvert u-v_{\gamma_{\alpha}}(u)\rvert\lesssim{}u^{\alpha},

(5.26b)

\displaystyle\lvert 2r-(v-u)\rvert\lesssim{}\log(r-r_{+});

(5.26c)

in the region
\{r\geq v^{\alpha}\}\cap\{r\geq\frac{v}{4}\}
,

\displaystyle v+u\lesssim r\lesssim v;

(5.26d)

in the region
\{r\geq v^{\alpha}\}\cap\{r\leq\frac{v}{4}\}
,

\displaystyle u\sim v,\quad r\gtrsim v^{\alpha};

(5.26e)

in the region
\{r\leq v^{\alpha}\}
,

\displaystyle v\sim\tau.

(5.26f)

On
\Sigma_{\tau_{0}}
, for
r
large,

\displaystyle\bigg|r^{-1}v-2-\frac{4Mr^{-1}}{r_{-}-r_{+}}\log\frac{(r-r_{-})^{r_{-}}}{(r-r_{+})^{r_{+}}}\bigg|\lesssim r^{-1}.

(5.27)

Our analysis starts from deriving the precise asymptotic profile of the
(m,\mathfrak{s})
mode of the spin
+\mathfrak{s}
component. We first make an assumption on the initial data of this mode towards
\rho\to+\infty
.

Assumption 5.6

(Initial data assumption to order
i
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
, let
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\lvert m\rvert\leq\mathfrak{s}
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}
be defined as in Proposition
3.19
. Assume on
\Sigma_{\tau_{0}}
that there are constants
\mathfrak{q}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
,
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
0\leq D_{0}<\infty
such that for all
0\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
and
\rho\geq 10M
,

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{\rho}^{i^{\prime}}\big(r^{-2\mathfrak{s}-2}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}-r^{-2\mathfrak{s}-3}\mathfrak{q}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\big)(\tau_{0},\rho)\Big|\lesssim D_{0}\rho^{-2\mathfrak{s}-3-\beta-i^{\prime}}.

(5.28)

5.2.1.
Sharp decay for
(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}
in
\{r\geq v^{\alpha}\}

To being with, we utilize equation (
3.192
) for
\ell=\mathfrak{s}
,
s=\mathfrak{s}
which reads

\displaystyle-\mu Y\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}-\frac{2(\mathfrak{s}+1)(r^{3}-3Mr^{2}+a^{2}r+a^{2}M)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}={}

\displaystyle(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}},

(5.29)

and, a simple scaling for the above equation (
5.29
) yields

\displaystyle-\mu Y(\mu^{\mathfrak{s}+1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}})={}

\displaystyle\mu^{\mathfrak{s}+1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-\mathfrak{s}-\frac{3}{2}}G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}.

(5.30)

Here,

\displaystyle G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}=

\displaystyle(2(\mathfrak{s}+1)(2\mathfrak{s}+1)M-2iam\mathfrak{s})(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}+(\mathfrak{s}+1)\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(a^{2}\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}(rV+O(r^{-1}))\big(\mathbf{P}_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(a^{2}\sin\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})\big)+O(r^{-1})(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}

(5.31)

which follows from
(
3.3
)
and (
3.11
).

For future applications, we rewrite
G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}
into a different form. First, the definition of
\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}
in Proposition
3.19
implies

\displaystyle V(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}\sim{r^{-2}}\big(\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}+O(1)(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}\big).

(5.32)

Combining (
5.32
), Proposition
2.13
and the definition of
\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(i)}
in Definition
3.9
, we have

\displaystyle G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}=

\displaystyle(2(\mathfrak{s}+1)(2\mathfrak{s}+1)M-2iam\mathfrak{s})(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{s}+1)a^{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}c_{m,\ell}^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{\mathfrak{s}}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}

\displaystyle+O(1)\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(rV)(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}+O(1)(rV)(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}+O(r^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(2(\mathfrak{s}+1)(2\mathfrak{s}+1)M-2iam\mathfrak{s})(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{s}+1)a^{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}c_{m,\ell}^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{\mathfrak{s}}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}

\displaystyle+O(r^{-1})\Big(\sum_{\ell=\mathfrak{s}+1}^{\mathfrak{s}+2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(1)})_{m,\ell}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}\Big).

(5.33)

Further, we have from the above formula that for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,

\displaystyle V^{j}G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}\sim r^{-1-j}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(rV)^{\leq j}\tilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}+\sum_{\ell=\mathfrak{s}+1}^{\mathfrak{s}+2}\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(rV)^{\leq j}(\hat{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(1)})_{m,\ell}+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}^{\leq 1}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}\Big).

(5.34)

In view of (
5.19
),
we have

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\lim\limits_{\rho\to+\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\frac{2}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s})(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+1)}\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{+\mathfrak{s}}^{(0)})_{m,\ell}(\tau_{0},\rho)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\frac{2}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s})(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\big)\Big)(\tau_{0},\rho),

(5.35)

hence we are able to calculate the integral of
G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}
along future null infinity by (
5.19
):

\displaystyle\hskip-17.22217pt\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to+\infty}G_{+\mathfrak{s},m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(2(\mathfrak{s}+1)(2\mathfrak{s}+1)M-2iam\mathfrak{s})\int_{\tau_{0}}^{+\infty}\lim\limits_{\rho\to+\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\mathfrak{s}}(\tau,\rho)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-(\mathfrak{s}+1)a^{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+2}c_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\lim\limits_{\rho\to+\infty}(\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}})_{m,\ell}(\tau_{0},\rho)

\displaystyle-2ia\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{s}+1)\sum_{\mathfrak{s}\leq\ell\leq\mathfrak{s}+1}b_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\frac{2}{(\ell-\mathfrak{s})(\ell+\mathfrak{s}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\lim\limits_{\rho\to\infty}\mathbf{P}_{m,\ell}^{+\mathfrak{s}}\Big(\hat{\mathcal{V}}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-\frac{1}{2}\big(2a\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}-2ia\mathfrak{s}\cos\theta\Phi_{+\mathfrak{s}}\big)\Big)(\tau_{0},\rho).

(5.36)

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}=0,1,2
. Assume the initial data assumption
5.6
holds to order
0
, and the initial energy
\textbf{I}^{k^{\prime},\delta,\pm\mathfrak{s}}_{\text{total},\tau_{0}}<+\infty
for a sufficiently small
\delta>0
and some suitably large integer
k^{\prime}
. Then for
\alpha
sufficiently close to
1
and
\delta=\delta(\alpha)
sufficiently small, there exists an
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\delta)>0
such that in the region
r\geq v^{\alpha}
,
